Used by the calendar fill (`paycalc-roster-hint.js`), `writeFormData` (non-zero only) and the coordinator's decimal split / Saturday cap
- `decimalToHM(val)` — split decimal hours → `{h, m}` with a 60→next-hour float guard (e.g. 7.999 → 8h 00m); returns null for negative/non-finite. The single source for the live "= 7h 30m" preview AND the on-blur "7.5 → 7 hrs 30 mins" split (was duplicated inline in `paycalc-app.js`)
- `HM_PAIRS` · `isDataEmpty(d)` — the hrs/mins field pairs and the "nothing entered" test, homed here for `paycalc-fill-year.js` (see that entry)

### `paycalc-breakdown.js`
The two PURE HTML builders for the pay-result card, extracted from `calculate()` in `paycalc-app.js` (v18.30, review item 20). `calculate()` used to interleave DOM reads, pay maths, and result-markup string-building; the markup now lives here, written + unit-testable independent of those phases. No DOM, no Firebase — only dependency is `fmt` from `paycalc-format.js`. Output is byte-identical to the old inline templates (a mechanical extraction, not a redesign). Each builder takes a plain params object whose field names match the caller's locals, so `paycalc-app.js`'s call site is a shorthand object literal. Tested by `paycalc-breakdown.test.mjs`.
- `fmtHrsMins(h)` — decimal hours → "Nh"/"Nh Mm" (the per-row hours label; was the inline `fh` in `calculate()`)
- `buildSummaryRows(d)` — the `#summary` estimate rows: Regular/Total pay → back pay → HPP → pension → tax → NI → Student Loan (`d.slLines`, pre-rendered) → estimated take-home
- `buildActualComparison({actual, estimate})` (v22.07) — the estimate-vs-payslip table: one row per payslip line the member typed (an absent figure is not a £0 claim; net alone renders nothing — the verdict line owns that case), difference signed actual−estimate, dash on an exact match, and it EXPLAINS NOTHING — cause words are banned by test. Inputs autosave into the period blob as `actualGross`/`actualTax`/`actualNi`/`actualPension` (paycalc-form-data — same null-when-blank rule as `actualNet`; none joins `isDataEmpty`), and `writeFormData` OPENS the disclosure when restoring saved figures so they never land in a closed section
- `buildActualCheck(actual, estimated, used?)` — the payslip-vs-estimate reconciliation line. `used` = `{ taxCode, recognised, pension, ytdFrom? }` the estimate APPLIED (`ytdFrom`, v24.89, only when the cumulative method ran: a large gap then names the payslip the Year to Date figures count from); when given, a gap over £2 names them first — and a code the engine does not recognise is named as unrecognised, with a zero-not-O hint where an O was typed, never as the code in use (v24.89) (v24.87 — the close band the code, before the Year to Date advice; the large band code and pension, before hours/absence/back pay). Only a plain letters-and-digits code is quoted, so nothing typed reaches the page as markup. `buildProvChips(d)` — the provisional-figure chips (`''` when there are none). Both pure, both cited by `.claude/rules/paycalc.md`.
- `buildBreakdownRows(d)` — the `#bdBody` full breakdown: one row per pay component present (Mon–Fri + London always; premiums/OT/RDW/training/adjustment/notes/extras guarded on > 0)

### `paycalc-form-data.js`
The per-period form ↔ saved-data **round trip** for `paycalc.html`, extracted from `paycalc-app.js` at v19.11.
- `emptyPeriodData()` — the saved-period schema. **Deliberately carries no `pension` key:** an absent key and an explicit `null` both mean "apply the period default" (`writeFormData` restores only when `!= null`), while a stored `0` survives as the genuine salary-sacrifice opt-out. Adding `pension: 0` here would re-introduce the pre-v16.84 defect for every cleared period.
- `readFormData({ adjNegative, periodDefaultPension })` — form → object, for autosave. Both dependencies are **injected** rather than imported: `adjNegative` because the ± sign is coordinator state (the sign button, the typed-negative handler and `clearPeriod` all mutate it), and `periodDefaultPension` because the v18.43 self-heal needs this period's default × pro-rate. Injection is what keeps the module testable with no coordinator state and no module mocks.
- `writeFormData(d)` → `{ adjNegative }` — object → form, for load/clear. **Returns** the sign the caller should adopt instead of writing a closure variable. The caller must call `clearRosterSuggestedAll()` FIRST (it used to be this function's opening statement); importing `paycalc-roster-hint.js` here would drag its seven-module graph into an otherwise document-only unit and force every test through mocks.
- **Why the module exists:** these ~70 lines produced four money-affecting defects in four releases, all the same shape — a value the form could legitimately hold being persisted as something it does not mean (blank pension → permanent £0, overstating take-home ~£147/period; garbage mid-edit doing the same at v16.84; `actualNet` repeating it at v18.42; the v18.43 default self-heal). Every one was fixed by adding a comment, because the logic sat inside a 1,963-line coordinator with no seam to test through. The bug class is a round-trip **asymmetry**, which a write→read→write test catches and a comment cannot. Tested by `paycalc-form-data.test.mjs`.
- `settingFieldsWrite(raw, formNow, fields)` (whole-app review, Nov 2026) — what a SETTINGS control (student loan, pension) may write into the viewed payslip's blob: the saved blob with ONLY the named fields replaced, or `null` when nothing is saved or it will not parse. The old path ran the whole autosave, which persisted the calendar's display-only suggestion as entered hours (invariant 11) and wrote a fresh blob over saved entries it could not read. `null` means write nothing.
- `pensionToStore(raw, periodDefaultPension)` (72-hour review) — THE rule for what a pension field's text is stored as: blank/garbage → null, a typed 0 kept, the period default (±0.005) → null so the payslip keeps following `PENSION_STEPS`. Shared by `readFormData` and `confirmSettings` (paycalc-settings.js), whose private `parseFloat(raw) || 0` froze the default onto a payslip and read a pasted "£151.86" as a £0 opt-out
- Imports: `paycalc-inputs.js` (hmPair/numVal), `roster-data.js` (parseSmartFloatOrNull)

### `paycalc-inputs.js`
The DOM-pure form-field input helpers extracted from `paycalc-app.js`'s `init()` (v18.60, review item 10 — continue focused coordinator extraction). They read/normalise the numbers a member types into the hours & figures fields, and own the live "= 7h 30m" decimal hint. They depend ONLY on `document` plus imported pure helpers (`parseSmartFloat`/`parseSmartFloatOrNull` from `roster-data.js`, `clampMinute`/`decimalToHM` from `paycalc-format.js`) — NO coordinator closure state — so they lift out of `init()` cleanly and are unit-testable in Node against a fake `document`. The event WIRING that USES them (`autoDecimalHours`, `onHhMm`) stays in `paycalc-app.js` because it closes over `calculate()`/`autosave()`/period state; it just calls these primitives. Tested by `paycalc-inputs.test.mjs`.
- `numVal(id)` — parse a field's value with `parseSmartFloat` (strips iOS smart-hyphens/quotes); missing element / empty → 0
- `numValOr(id, fallback)` — like `numVal` but returns `fallback` for an unparseable/empty value (NOT 0) — the signed Year-to-Date / pension fields need an explicit floor so a stray "." doesn't cascade £0/£NaN
- `intVal(id)` — non-negative integer read (`Math.max(0, parseInt || 0)`) for the hour/minute fields
- `hmPair(hId, mId)` → `{h, m}` (72-hour review) — an hrs/mins pair as whole hours + minutes, reading a DECIMAL still in the hours box ("7.5", not yet blurred) exactly as the blur split will write it (the minutes box replaced by the fraction). Read by `hhmmDec` and `readFormData`, so the live £, the autosave and the post-blur fields agree — parseInt's 7 used to be priced and stored until blur
- `hhmmDec(hId, mId)` — combine an hrs + mins field pair into decimal hours (via `hmPair`)
- `clampMins(mId)` — clamp an out-of-range minutes field into [0, 59], rewriting ONLY when out of range (preserves a typed "05")
- `_decHintEl(hId, make)` — find (or lazily create when `make`) the `.hhmm-dec-hint` element under an hours field's `.hhmm-wrap`; null when the markup is absent
- `decPreview(hId)` — write the live "= Nh Mm" hint while a decimal is being typed, hide it for a whole number
- `wireIosTap(el, action)` — guarded touch/click wiring for a control that is tapped while the soft keyboard is up (v21.66). iOS cancels the synthesised click when the tap dismisses the keyboard, so a bare click listener "doesn't always work" — measured first on the ± sign button, re-reported on the roster fill controls. Fires from `touchend` (the path iOS cannot cancel), suppresses/swallows the synthesised click so the action never runs twice, and gates on movement so a scroll flick ending on a full-width destructive button ("Replace with calendar values") is never a tap. Three call sites in paycalc-app.js: the fill button, the `#rosterRows` delegation, the ± sign button

### `paycalc-backpay-state.js`

Which **FIGURE** ends up in which back-pay box, and which of them the member may change (v19.93). Pure; tested by `paycalc-backpay-state.test.mjs`.

- `BP_FIELDS` (the saved-state schema — DOM id ↔ blob key), `readBpFields` / `bpFieldWrites` (the round trip), `resolveAuthoritativeRates`, `allRatesOnRecord`, `resolvePaidInPeriod`.
- **Why it exists.** `paycalc-backpay.js` was the ONLY module in the paycalc family with no test file of its own — four of its ten exports untested, including `calcBackPay`, which produces the lump sum. The per-period accrual was covered; the ASSEMBLY around it was not, and all five of its recorded defects live there. **Every one is the same shape: a money figure comes out wrong and nothing says so.** (It has a test file of its own since v23.63 — `paycalc-backpay.test.mjs` drives `calcBackPay` end to end; these three decisions stay extracted, because they are rules rather than wiring.)
- **Three rules that must survive an edit.** `bpFieldWrites` returns an entry for EVERY field — a blob silent about a field must BLANK it, not leave the last year's number (that is the clear-then-apply pair collapsed into one pass, so there is nothing left to keep in step). The lock decision never reads a box's contents, and a locked box is WRITTEN with its figure. And `resolvePaidInPeriod` must not return 0 while any candidate exists — the ORDER of its ladder is a judgement, never-zero is the safety property.

### `paycalc-pension.js`

**WHEN** a member was in the pension scheme, not merely **whether** (v21.78). Pure — no DOM, no storage, no imports; tested by `paycalc-pension.test.mjs`.

- `parsePensionTimeline(raw)`, `serialisePensionTimeline(timeline)`, `isOptedOutAt(timeline, pNum)`, `withPensionChange(timeline, pNum, out)`, `withOptOutStartAt(timeline, pNum)`, `withRejoinAt(timeline, pNum)`, `optOutStartsAt(timeline)`, `migrateLegacyOptOut(legacyOptedOut, firstPNumOfCurrentTaxYear)`.
- **The card makes two EDITS, not one boolean written twice (v21.80).** `withPensionChange` records a change AT a payslip, which is the wrong primitive for either control, and wiring it to both produced two silent failures in opposite directions — each of which corrected itself out of sight on the next reload, which is why a manual pass saw neither. Re-picking a LATER payslip did nothing at all (the earlier change still stood in front of it, so the select read one payslip and every figure used another); and un-ticking ERASED the spell rather than ending it, because the handler dated it to the spell's own start — so the rejoin this module exists for was unreachable from the page whose hint instructs the member to record one. `withOptOutStartAt` MOVES the current spell's start (a spell that has already ended is left alone — that is a NEW spell), `withRejoinAt` ENDS it at the payslip on screen, and naming the start payslip or an earlier one erases instead, which is the commoner action: undoing a tick while still looking at the payslip you ticked on. Both are idempotent — every keystroke in the pension field re-states the pair through `saveSettings`.
- **Why it exists.** v21.64 added "I'm not in the pension scheme" as a single boolean. The setting was right and the SHAPE was the money bug, found by external review and then reproduced: a 2025/26 payslip with real hours went from a £160.78 deduction to £0.00, and its take-home rose **£115.92**, because the member ticked a box in August 2026. Two individually-good designs collide — `readFormData` stores `null` when the pension equals the period default (so the period keeps healing as the app learns the real historic rates), and the opt-out made that default £0 for every payslip there has ever been. The Settings hint promised "payslips from when you WERE contributing keep their own amount"; that held only for the rare payslip carrying an explicitly-typed non-default figure.
- **Why a timeline and not a second date field.** UK auto-enrolment re-enrols opted-out staff roughly every three years, so "out from P56, back in from P95" is the expected shape within the life of this data, not a later refinement. A single "opted out from" field models the first event and then produces the same class of bug in reverse — un-ticking would hand the scheme default back to the months she genuinely was not contributing.
- **Three rules that must survive an edit.** An empty timeline means CONTRIBUTING (a parse failure, a cleared key and a member who never touched the setting all land on the answer that changes no stored figure). A payslip before the first change is never affected by it — the lookup takes the last change at or BEFORE the payslip and never falls back to the newest state. And `parsePensionTimeline` is a trust boundary that refuses WHOLE: a half-read timeline is a perfectly valid timeline that says something else, and it moves the boundary between contributing and not with nothing on screen changing.
- The consumer side is one function: `getPensionDefault(pObj)` in `paycalc-settings.js` (via the period-aware `isPensionOptedOut(pObj)`), which the field default, `calculate()`'s fallback, the HPP non-premium estimate and the year summary all already route through.

### `paycalc-inventory.js`
What a stored pay key **is**, and what a set of them adds up to (v22.14, external review). The one authority: `summarise` in `paycalc-transfer.js`, the card's itemised list and the damage preflight all ask it, so they cannot disagree about what a key means.
- **Why it exists.** `summarise` derived "N payslips across M tax years" from a hand-written regex naming five of the nine per-tax-year key types. It could not see `hpp_actual`, `hpp_inc`, `ytd_src` or `setup_<year>`, so a tax year known only by one of those was invisible — on the card whose entire job is to say what you are about to carry to a new phone. Nothing failed; the number was just smaller than the truth. That is this repo's recurring defect, *prose restating a fact that lives somewhere else*, so the answer is one classifier plus a parity test that drives the REAL key builders rather than a longer regex.
- `KEY_KINDS` — the ordered table: `test` on the key TAIL, member-facing `label`, and `structured` (the value is a JSON blob). Order matters: per-year forms sit above their member-level namesakes, because several kinds carry both (a pre-per-year key is still readable on any device that has not run its migration, and a backup taken there carries it). There is deliberately **no `perYear` flag** — `taxYearOf` answers that from the tail, and a second declaration of the same fact is the bug.
- `classifyPayKey(tail)` → `{kind, label, taxYear, period, structured}`. **An unrecognised key is `unknown`, never dropped and never guessed** — a backup from a later app version contains kinds this one has not heard of, and classification is for DESCRIBING, never for filtering.
- `describePayKey(tail)` — "payslip 16", "back pay 2026/27". A member is never shown a storage key.
- `inventoryOf(keys, prefix)` → `{periods, taxYears, groups, unknown, keys}`. `taxYears` is a sorted LIST, not a count: naming 2025/26 and 2026/27 answers "is my old year in here?", which "2" does not, and that is the question somebody opens this card to ask.
- `inventoryLines(inv)` — the inventory as lines a member reads, pure so the wording is testable without a DOM.
- `damagedEntries(entries, prefix)` — which STRUCTURED entries will not parse. **Only a kind declared `structured` is ever parsed**: `code` holds `1257L` and `rate` holds `21.49`, and parsing those would report every healthy device as corrupt. It **reports rather than refuses**, which is where it departs from the review that asked for it — a backup is routinely the member's only remaining copy, so refusing the whole file over one unparseable payslip hands them nothing, while the damage restores visibly and recoverably (`parseSavedPeriod` surfaces a corrupt period rather than dropping it, the standing no-silent-caps posture). A structurally wrong FILE is a different thing and is still refused outright by `validateBackup`.
- Tested by `paycalc-inventory.test.mjs` (part of `test:hygiene`), whose first contract scans the source tree for every `${pcPrefix()}…` builder and asserts each produces a recognised kind. It reads SOURCE rather than importing because two of the three key-building modules reach the gstatic SDK and cannot load in Node — and scanning is the stronger check anyway: it sees a builder in a module that does not exist yet.

### `paycalc-transfer.js`
The pay-data **backup format and import rules** (v19.16). Every function is pure — no localStorage, no files, no clipboard — because the import path decides whether to overwrite a member's entire pay history, so its rules have to be testable without a browser. The DOM half lives in `paycalc-transfer-card.js`.
- **Why it exists:** pay-calculator data lives in `localStorage`, which is per-**origin**. It therefore does not follow a member from `garethdavidmiller.github.io` to `myb-roster.web.app`, and does not survive a new phone or cleared browser data. Everything else the app holds is in Firestore and moves with the account; this is the one thing that deliberately does not (pay data is kept off the server — AUTH_ARCHITECTURE.md, the PAYSLIP_ACTUALS decision). A Firestore transfer doc was rejected for that reason, and an iframe/`postMessage` bridge because Chrome partitions storage by top-level site.
- `BACKUP_FORMAT` / `BACKUP_VERSION` — the blob's identity and schema version. A blob from a NEWER version is refused outright rather than half-understood.
- `selectBackupKeys(allKeys, prefix)` — a **prefix scan**, not an enumerated list: a hand-maintained list would silently drop any key type added later (the same failure mode as the SW precache list). `DEVICE_KEYS` are excluded — they describe the browser, not the member. Sorted, so an unchanged backup is byte-identical between runs.
- `summarise(keys, prefix)` → `{periods, taxYears, keys}` — stated in payslips and tax years, the units a member recognises. Since v22.14 it delegates to `inventoryOf` (see `paycalc-inventory.js` for the undercount it replaced). Its flat return shape is deliberate — it is written into every blob as `counts`, which app versions that do not exist yet will read.
- `buildBackup({entries, member, slug, appVersion, exportedAt, prefix})` — values are carried as **raw strings, never re-parsed**, so a backup survives a paycalc schema change it predates. Records the SOURCE `slug` so re-keying is unambiguous, and (v22.14) `dataGeneration` — the shape of the VALUES, which is a different question from `version`, the shape of the FILE, and has always moved independently of it. See `PAY_DATA_GENERATION` in `paycalc-migrations.js`.
- `validateBackup(text, {currentSlug})` → `{ok:false,error}` | `{ok:true,blob,unnamespaced,srcSlug,counts,inventory,damaged}` — `srcSlug` is the slug every rule CHECKED, and the card re-keys from it rather than re-reading `blob.slug`. **An "ownerless" blob whose keys name a member (`keyOwnerSlug`) is refused, and a present non-string slug is refused as damaged (72-hour review)** — stripping `slug` from another member's backup used to pass it as legacy and the REPLACE then wiped the importer's history. Returns a result rather than throwing, so each refusal renders in the member's own terms. **Fails closed on identity FIRST (v19.17):** an empty `currentSlug` refuses everything, before any other rule. Every rule here is about whose data this is, and the caller's namespace in that state is the bare `myb_pc_`, which spans EVERY member on a shared device — reachable when the session name is no longer on the roster (a leaver, a rename), since `getLoggedMember()` then returns null and the namespace never activates. Verified in a browser both ways. **The trust boundary:** a backup is a file the member obtained from somewhere, so restoring it must never be a way to write arbitrary localStorage — only the paycalc key shape, never a `DEVICE_KEYS` flag, only string values, and every key must sit under the slug the blob claims. **Identity rule = option A:** a backup belonging to a DIFFERENT member is refused ("Sign in as them to restore it") — staff share devices, which is precisely why the per-member namespacing exists. Legacy pre-namespacing data has no owner recorded; it is accepted but flagged `unnamespaced` so the caller must confirm. **Two content questions were added at v22.14** (the shape questions above all concern the file): a blob whose `dataGeneration` is NEWER than this app's is refused — the values would restore faithfully and then be silently misread — while an absent field means "no later than us", which is true of every backup predating it; and the **semantic preflight** returns `damaged`, naming the structured entries that will not parse, without refusing (see `damagedEntries`).
- `rekeyEntries(entries, fromSlug, toSlug)` — `myb_pc_ytd_pay_2026_27` cannot be split into slug + tail by inspection (is the slug `ytd`?), which is why the source slug is recorded rather than guessed.
- `backupFilename(member, isoDate)` — recognisable in a Downloads folder months later.
- Tested by `paycalc-transfer.test.mjs` (part of `test:hygiene`).
- **What keeps it correct as the pay calculator changes (v19.28).** This module carries whatever localStorage holds, so a new pay FEATURE needs no edit here — what breaks it is a change to key SHAPE, and both ways that happens are silent (an un-namespaced key is invisible to `selectBackupKeys`; a key tail outside `[A-Za-z0-9_]+` fails `KEY_RE` and is dropped while the member is told "Restored"). `paycalc-key-parity.test.mjs` guards the shape statically — the transfer tests here cannot, because they exercise the rules against keys they invent, never the app's real ones.

- `applyRestore({get,set,del,existing,entries})` → `{ok, reason?, restored}` (v19.84, external review P1) — the all-or-nothing REPLACE over a storage with no transactions. The previous inline ladder snapshotted only the keys that ALREADY EXISTED, so a rollback could not undo a key the backup had **created**: it left that key in place, checked the original keys, found them intact, and told the member **"nothing was changed"** over a pay history that had silently gained a period — the exact half-merged state the card's own comments call the worst outcome. The unit of work is now the **UNION** of what is there and what is arriving, and rolling back a creation means DELETING it. Second half: a surplus key that will not delete is a **failed replace**, not a success — `lsDel` swallows storage errors just as `lsSet` does, so the removals are read back too. The io is INJECTED rather than importing ls.js, because the only interesting cases need a storage that fails on demand, which a browser will not do to order.

### `paycalc-transfer-card.js`
The DOM half of the "Move Your Pay Data" card on `paycalc.html` (v19.16; renamed v19.18 — the original "Back up your pay data" collided with the "Pay Rise Back Pay" card two rows above it, and v19.19 shortened it further. The card serves two purposes and the title names only one, so the HINT carries the other and must keep leading with "Keep a copy") — file/clipboard plumbing and the confirmations before a restore. Owns no rule: every decision comes from `paycalc-transfer.js`.
- `initTransferCard()` — safe no-op when the page has no card. Renders the summary as an ITEMISED list (v22.14 — "On this device:" over `#ptInventory`; the old single line folded the pension history, back-pay figures, Year-to-Date totals and payslip comparison into "plus your settings", so a member could not tell whether the thing they cared about was in there), wires Download / Copy as text / Restore from a file / Restore from pasted text, and honours a `#payTransferCard` deep link from Settings through `overlay.js`'s `openCardNamedByHash` (v24.86 — the routine was written here at v21.50 and moved when the Year to Date card needed the same thing): it **expands** the card, because a bare fragment jump would land on a collapsed header, and holds it at the top of the screen while the calculator keeps laying out — which otherwise drifted the card from the top of the phone viewport down to y=681 of 844. The reasoning, including why the cancel is a gesture and not a scrollY delta, is in that helper's header.
- Every control is disabled when the member cannot be identified, the **paste pair included** — omitting it is what left the destructive path live (v19.17). `#payTransferCard` is also in `paycalc.css`'s print hide-list, like every other occasional card.
- **Restore is a REPLACE, never a merge** — a half-merged pay history is worse than either version. The ladder is: `validateBackup` → confirm if `unnamespaced` → confirm the replace (`danger`) → snapshot → **write** → **verify by reading back** → remove only the surplus → reload.
- **The replace dialog names BOTH sides** (v22.14). Until then it stated one count about the incoming file and nothing at all about the pay history being destroyed — which is the half the member is deciding about — and a payslip count described neither side, since a namespace also holds pension history, back-pay figures and Year-to-Date totals no payslip count mentions. Any damaged entries are named in the same dialog, before consent, and a backup with damage but nothing to replace gets a dialog of its own rather than being written silently.
- **Write FIRST, delete the surplus AFTER, and verify in between (v19.17).** The obvious order (wipe, then write) makes the common failure catastrophic: `lsSet` SWALLOWS a storage error (`ls.js`, for iOS private mode), so on a device that has stopped accepting writes the wipe succeeded, the write silently did nothing, and the member was told “Restored” as their pay history disappeared — measured in a browser. Because `lsSet` cannot report failure, a `try/catch` around it is dead code; reading the values back is the only way to know. A failed write now rolls back from the snapshot and changes nothing. Existing keys are **removed, not blanked**: a key set to `''` leaves a value the app would later try to parse (an empty period reads as corrupt, not absent), and a key the backup does not contain must genuinely disappear or the "replace" is a merge wearing a replace's label.
- localStorage cannot be written atomically, so a mid-write failure reports honestly ("stopped accepting data after N of M items") rather than claiming success.
- Imports: `ls.js`, `paycalc-migrations.js`, `paycalc-settings.js` (`getLoggedMember`), `roster-data.js`, `overlay.js` (`confirmDialog`), `paycalc-transfer.js`, `paycalc-inventory.js`.

### `paycalc-help.js`
Pure data module — help/tooltip text for the pay calculator (v11.40).
- `HELP_CONTENT` object with keys: `hours`, `settings`, `accuracy`, `hpp`, `backpay`
- Imports `TAX_YEARS` and `GRADES` from `paycalc-calc.js` for the figures in tip text
- No DOM, no Firebase — safe to import anywhere

### `paycalc-migrations.js`
- `hppIncKey(ty)` — the per-tax-year opt-in flag ('1' = the member ticked "add my HPP to the January take-home").
- `ytdSrcKey(ty)` — per-tax-year SOURCE payslip of the Year to Date figures (internal period num); anchors the cumulative tax method to source+1 (v17.98)
localStorage key constants and data migration logic for the pay calculator (v11.40).
- `SK` — object of top-level localStorage key strings, rebuilt in place when the namespace changes
- `pcPrefix()` — the active per-member key prefix (`myb_pc_` or `myb_pc_<slug>_`); every key builder and `SK` derives from it (v14.11)
- `PAY_DATA_GENERATION` (v22.14) — the GENERATION of the stored data, i.e. what shape the VALUES are in, as against `BACKUP_VERSION`'s question about the shape of the FILE. Every migration in this module changed values without touching the file format, so the two are genuinely different axes. A backup carries it because the only other available signal is the device's own one-shot migration flags — which describe the browser, are excluded from every backup, and would therefore say "already migrated" about data that has never been near this device. **Nothing consumes it yet, deliberately**: every live migration over backed-up keys is DATA-gated (`if (sl === 'postgrad')`, `if (timeline === null && flag)`) and so already re-runs correctly over restored values, while the device-flag-gated ones touch only legacy device-local keys no backup carries. It is recorded now so the first migration that is NOT data-gated has an honest anchor rather than inferring one from the app version string. Bump it when a migration changes what a stored value MEANS — not when a key is merely added.
- `setPaycalcNamespace(memberName)` — activate the per-member namespace (called from `runMigrations`); falsy name → unnamespaced legacy keys (v14.11). `paycalc-app.js` refuses (the unsupported-role card) BEFORE `runMigrations` when the session name is not on the roster, so the coordinator never reaches the falsy-name branch (72-hour review)
- `keyOwnerSlug(key)` → slug | null — the member a `myb_pc_<slug>_…` key belongs to, or null for genuinely unnamespaced legacy data. Exported (72-hour review) so `validateBackup` can refuse an "ownerless" backup whose keys name a member — the same test the ownership prompt has used since v14.27
- `periodKey(pNum)` — key builder for period data (takes period number)
- `parseSavedPeriod(raw)` (v16.66) — PURE saved-period JSON decoder → `{ data, error }`: null/empty → `{null,false}`, valid → `{obj,false}`, malformed → `{null,true}`. `readSavedPeriod(pNum)` composes `lsGet(periodKey(pNum))` + `parseSavedPeriod`. Single decoder shared by `paycalc-backpay.js` and `paycalc-hpp.js` so a corrupt saved period is SURFACED (a visible "couldn't read N periods — may be too low" note), never dropped silently (no-silent-caps). Tested by `paycalc-migrations.test.mjs`.
- `hppEstKey(ty)`, `hppActualKey(ty)`, `hppModeKey(ty)`, `ytdPayKey(ty)`, `ytdTaxKey(ty)` — key builders that take a tax-year object `ty` (with `.label` property, e.g. `'2025/26'`); `hppModeKey` holds the HPP amount-source state; `bpKey(ty)` — the back-pay card's per-tax-year autosave blob key
- `NOTICE_YTD_KEY` — device-level "Year to Date notice shown" flag (deliberately unnamespaced)
- `memberSlug(name)` — `'G. Miller'` → `'gmiller'`; the namespace segment builder (exported since v19.16 for `paycalc-transfer.js`, which needs the slug WITHOUT activating a namespace)
- `DEVICE_KEYS` — the unnamespaced device-level flags (migration guards, "seen this notice"). Exported since v19.16 so the backup path can exclude them: carrying `myb_pc_ns_migrated` onto a fresh device would suppress the legacy-ownership prompt on a device that genuinely needs it. Built from `_ACTIVE_DEVICE_KEYS` + `_RETIRED_DEVICE_KEYS`.
- `RETIRED_DEVICE_KEYS` (v19.37) — device flags the app no longer WRITES but which still sit on real devices. Deleting one outright was a live bug: retiring the welcome lightbox removed `myb_pc_pay_welcome_shown` at v19.36, so `_hasUnnamespacedPaycalcData` began reading that stale flag as unnamespaced member data and the "Is this your pay data?" prompt fired at members with no legacy data — worst for anyone who first used paycalc AFTER v14.11 namespacing, whose `myb_pc_ns_migrated` guard was never set because the prompt had never run. **Retiring a feature that owned a device flag means MOVING its key here, never deleting it.**
- `isActualsDev(member)` / `readPayslipActuals()` / `writePayslipActuals(map)` / `clearPayslipActuals()` — the developer-only device-local payslip-actuals overlay (gated to the developer account; data imported per-device, never served — see CLAUDE.md → Example payslips)
- `runMigrations({ getPeriods, getLoggedMember, getPensionDefault })` — runs all one-time data migrations, then migrates this member's shared data into their namespace and activates it; receives deps as params to avoid circular imports with `paycalc-app.js`
- `_migrateCeaKeys` — internal migration (old CEA keys → grade-neutral format)
- `hasPendingLegacyMigration(name)` / `resolveLegacyMigration(name, 'mine'|'fresh')` — shared-device ownership prompt (v14.25): `runMigrations` only activates the namespace; legacy/shared `myb_pc_*` data is claimed (`'mine'` moves it into the member segment) or discarded (`'fresh'`) only when the member resolves the `paycalc-lightboxes.js` prompt (✕ = decide later). Device-level keys (migration guards, "seen" flags) stay unnamespaced; the `myb_pc_ns_migrated` guard makes the prompt one-shot. Covered by `paycalc-migrations.test.mjs`

### `paycalc-calc.js`
Pure functions only — no DOM, no Firebase, no localStorage.
- All pay rate + statutory-threshold tables: `GRADES`, `TAX_YEARS`, and the per-tax-year maps `TAX_BY_YEAR`
  (PA/bands), `NI_BY_YEAR` (PT/UEL, weekly×4), `SL_BY_YEAR` (Student-Loan plan thresholds — **Plan 5 is
  absent from 2025/26**, not repayable until 6 Apr 2026), and `SCOTTISH_TAX_BY_YEAR` (6 bands).
- `computeSL(sacGross, plan, slByYear, skip)` — Student/Postgraduate Loan (v17.16 HMRC method: excess keeps
  its pence, only the FINAL deduction floors to £; penny-floored periodic threshold; verified against
  `PAYSLIP_ACTUALS.sl`). An undergraduate plan + a Postgraduate Loan repay together — the coordinator sums
  `computeSL(plan) + computeSL('postgrad')`.
- `computeTax(...)` handles the Scottish flat-rate codes `SD0`/`SD1`/`SD2`/`SD3` (intermediate/higher/advanced/top,
  v17.16) and applies the HMRC 50% overriding limit (PAYE reg 23) on both cumulative and non-cumulative paths.
  On the cumulative path it also returns `refund` — tax the recalculation says was over-collected —
  WITHOUT netting it into `tax` (72-hour review): the deduction stays £0 and the summary says a refund
  may be due, because a refund folded into take-home would let one mistyped Year to Date figure invent money.
- `AWARD_RATES` + `awardRatesFor(grade, tyLabel)` — authoritative per-grade, per-tax-year pay-award
  rates (`{ rate, pre }`: settled rate + the prior-year "old" rate; the mid-year applied-from date is
  NOT stored here — it is single-sourced from that year's `londonAllowFrom` via `awardFromForYear`).
  Payslip-confirmed (CEA 2024/25 = £20.06, applied 24 Oct 2025). Used by
  `paycalc-backpay.js` (any year's old→new award, independent of the mutable `GRADES` default) and by
  `getRateForPeriod()`. Add one entry per grade each April.
- `getRateForPeriod(p, grade, tyLabel, settledRate)` — the rate for a period, honouring a MID-YEAR
  award step: returns `pre` for periods paid before `from`, else the settled rate (mirrors
  `getLondonAllowanceForPeriod`). Loaded into the rate field by `updateRateForPeriod(ty, p)`, so the
  main calculator matches the real payslip for historic pre-award periods.
- `awardFromForYear(tyLabel)` — the date a year's award was applied (single-sourced from that
  year's `londonAllowFrom`; the date is NOT stored on `AWARD_RATES`). `isPreAwardPeriod(p, grade,
  tyLabel)` — the one shared predicate for "period paid before its award", used by `getRateForPeriod`,
  `updateRateForPeriod` and `confirmSettings` (paycalc-settings.js) and the period hint in
  `paycalc-app.js`; the back-pay accrual caps arrears with `awardFromForYear` directly.
- `computeGross()`, `computeTax()`, `computeNI()`, `computeSL()` — `computeTax` also returns `code` (the code as read: upper case, no spaces, 1257L for a blank or all-spaces box; v24.87) and `recognised` (v24.89): an unrecognised code ("OT" with a letter O, "1182") is priced on the default allowance, so the payslip check names it as unrecognised rather than quoting it as applied
- `P_YR` (13 four-weekly periods a year) · `getLondonAllowanceForPeriod(p, ty)` — the London Allowance for a period, honouring the year's mid-year `londonAllowFrom` cut-over
- `PENSION_STEPS` — the per-era employee pension defaults, newest-first, each era's payslip provenance in a comment beside it. **Cited in six other documents and absent from this map until v21.63**, which is exactly the gap this file exists to close. `getPensionForPeriod(grade, payday)` resolves a payday to its era — the figure `VAL-PAY-003` is about.
- `calcBandedTax(taxable, bands, scale = 1)` — the shared band walker; `scale` multiplies every band top, which is how cumulative PAYE prices period N. `getThresholds(yearLabel)` → that year's `{tax, scottishTax, ni, sl, londonAllow}` table.
- `calcProRateFactor(startDate, periodStart, periodCutoff)` → 0–1 — the joiner fraction of a period (1 = full, 0 = not yet started); cited by `.claude/rules/roster-data.md` for new starters.
- `proRateDays(startDate, periodStart, periodCutoff)` → `{daysEmployed, totalDays}` (72-hour review) — the two counts behind that fraction, taken between WALL-CLOCK readings so a period containing the spring clock change no longer loses a joiner a day. Shared with the joiner notice (`paycalc-roster-hint.js`), which prints them.
- `HPP_FRACTION` = 4/52 — the Holiday Pay Premium multiplier, confirmed by Chiltern payroll.
- `RATE_125` / `RATE_150` / `RATE_300` — the overtime multipliers, declared once so a rate is never a literal at a call site.
- `getTaxYearForOffset(offset)` / `taxYearForPeriod(p)` — the TAX_YEARS entry for a P48-relative offset, or (the nullable variant, v17.22) for a period object with the `TAX_YEARS[0]` fallback. `taxYearForPeriod` single-sources the `p ? getTaxYearForOffset(p.num - 48) : TAX_YEARS[0]` idiom (the `- 48` anchor + null fallback) that recurred across the coordinator, HPP, back-pay, and settings modules.
- `capHours({effContr,satHrs,bhHrs})` — the Saturday/BH-vs-contracted cap cascade, single-sourced (was inline in computeGross/_varPayForPeriod/_accrueBackPayPeriod).
- Edit here for: rate changes, tax year rollover, NI threshold changes, a new annual pay award
- Covered by `paycalc.test.mjs` — run tests after any change here

### `paycalc-roster-suggestions.js`
Owns the override cache and the suggestion engine. No DOM access.
- Private state: `_overridesByDate`, `_overrideFetchToken`, `_overridesFetchState`
- `resetOverrides(newState)` — called by `onPeriodChange` on every period switch
- `fetchOverridesForPeriod(p, memberName)` — async Firestore fetch, returns Promise. Member-narrowed query (v18.24 — `where('memberName','==',…)` + the 28-day date range) using the `(memberName ASC, date ASC)` composite index; previously date-range-only + client-side member filter (the over-broad-read anti-pattern the AL-stats fix retired)
- `getRosterSuggestion(p, member)` — merges base roster + overrides, returns categorised totals; member is passed by caller (no localStorage access)
- `BARE_TRAINING_DAY_MINS` — 420. A Training day from the PATTERN has no times, so on a bank holiday it is priced as the contracted 7-hour day (owner, 7 Nov 2026) and the result's `defaulted7h.bh` says the figure is a default
- `getOverridesFetchState()` — returns the current async-fetch state (`checking`/`base-only`/`loaded`) for the suggestion UI
- `bhsForYear(year)` — bank-holiday date set for a year; also imported by `paycalc-periods.js`
- `_setOverridesForTest(map)` / `_addBhDateForTest(year, iso)` / `_removeBhDateForTest(year, iso)` — test-only hooks to inject overrides / adjust the BH set without Firestore
- Edit here for: overtime split rules, BH detection logic, override fetch behaviour
- Covered by `paycalc-roster-suggestions.test.mjs` — run with `node --experimental-test-module-mocks --test paycalc-roster-suggestions.test.mjs`

### `firebase-client.js`
Single Firestore initialisation point — import `db` and Firestore helpers from here, never from the Firebase CDN directly.
- `db` — initialised with `persistentLocalCache()` so all queries are backed by IndexedDB offline storage
- `COLLECTIONS` — frozen object mapping logical names to Firestore collection strings (`circulars`, `newsletters`, `clientErrors`, etc.). Use this instead of bare string literals to prevent typo-silent failures.
- Standard exports re-exported: `collection`, `query`, `where`, `orderBy`, `limit`, `getDocs`, `getDocsFromCache` (cache-only — the pre-session paint), `getDoc`, `addDoc`, `setDoc`, `deleteDoc`, `doc`, `serverTimestamp`, `writeBatch`, `runTransaction` (v17.02 — the links-app.js concurrency transaction), `onSnapshot`, `deleteField` (the Links restore)
- `isSafeStorageUrl` / `resolveDocumentOpenUrl` — re-exported from `storage-utils.js` (`officeViewerUrl` no longer is; the openers call `resolveDocumentOpenUrl`, which applies it)
- `requestPasswordReset(memberName)` / `getResetRequests()` / `clearResetRequest(memberName)` — the password-reset request queue: the public `requestPasswordReset` Function (login card) and the admin's read/clear on Operations (the clear is a GATED write since v24.63 — `writeWithClaimRetry` — and a failure is said in its row: an unknown outcome keeps the button off, since the gate would refuse a second Clear)
- `uploadHuddle` / `uploadCircular` / `uploadNewsletter` / `getLatestCircular` / `getLatestNewsletter` / `subscribeToLatestHuddle` — still exported here, and every importer is unchanged, but bound from `buildDocumentClient` since **v21.90**. The upload SEQUENCE and its reasoning live in `documents-client.js`; do not restate them here. (The bullets this line replaced had gone stale in the way that matters: they described `assertFileSignature` as failing OPEN on a read error, which it has not done since v14.99 — it fails CLOSED, and a security guard documented backwards is worse than one documented not at all.)
- `currentAuthUid()` (Oct 2026) — the signed-in uid or null, the `owner` the save stamps. `notif.js` re-saves at once when it differs from the uid the record was last saved under, so a device that changes hands owns its record now rather than after the 24h throttle (the rules let the SAME browser take a record over: same endpoint and keys).
- `savePushSubscription` / `deletePushSubscription` — Web Push subscription management. `deletePushSubscription` guards against empty endpoint (no-ops silently), and since v24.35 THROWS `push/no-owner-session` at once when no owner is signed in (no user, anonymous, or the PIN viewer) rather than spending a sign-out's 1.5s on a delete the rule refuses — `notif.js`'s `releaseDevicePush` then drops the browser subscription. The save is bounded by the account (`abandonOnSignOut`, v24.39), so a sign-out can no longer leave the bell on "Enabling…"; its document id is fixed, so an unconfirmed save cannot duplicate.
- `authBootstrap()` / `currentUserAfterBoot(timeoutMs)` — the ONE shared boot restore (v21.29). `authBootstrap` awaits the first auth emission, applies persistence by whether the restored user is the viewer, and memoises; `currentUserAfterBoot` is the bounded wait every caller with a deadline of its own uses, so three `onAuthStateChanged` subscriptions with three separate ceilings became one. `LATENCY.md` Phase 1 cites it; it was absent from this map until v21.63.
- `restoreMemberPersistence()` / `setViewerPersistence()` — the two halves of the persistence decision above, exported so `session.js` can re-arm the member chain AFTER shedding a viewer. **`setViewerPersistence` REJECTS rather than falling back**: every available fallback is longer-lived than session-only, so a quiet degrade would turn a shared-PC boundary into a permanent one. `AUTH_AND_SESSIONS.md` invariant 8.
- `signInWithCustomToken` — re-exported for the staff-PIN exchange (`calendar-access.js` mints through `unlockCalendarViewer`); cited by `PASSWORD_DESIGN.md`.
- `recordOriginUse({ countVisit, installed })` — the per-address migration counters (`analytics/origins`). **Two independent dedup gates**, never one: an account already counted this window that has just opened the INSTALLED app must still be counted as installed, or the metric freezes on whichever mode somebody used first — which is the entire question it exists to answer.
- `auth`, `authReady`, `signInWithEmailAndPassword`, `signOut`, `onAuthStateChanged`, `updatePassword`, `reauthenticateWithCredential`, `EmailAuthProvider`, `nameToEmail`, `normaliseSurname` — Firebase Auth (`normaliseSurname` is the shared surname→password derivation that `getSurname()` in `session.js` delegates to; `updatePassword`/`reauthenticateWithCredential`/`EmailAuthProvider` back the self-service password change, v18.63). **`authReady` is the boot-persistence decision, and it is VIEWER-AWARE (v21.21):** it resolves the restored user first (free — `setPersistence` already queues behind the SDK init, which IS the restore) and only then applies persistence: the shared Calendar viewer (`isViewerUser`, imported from `calendar-access-core.js` — the ONE copy of that predicate) re-asserts **session-only** persistence, everyone else gets the member chain (IndexedDB → localStorage → sessionStorage). It applied the member chain unconditionally until v21.21, and because `setPersistence` MIGRATES the current user between stores, ONE ordinary reload of an unlocked Calendar moved the shared viewer into IndexedDB — where it survived the browser closing, so the next person at a shared PC got the roster with no PIN. Proven and fixed in a real Chromium against the Auth emulator (`experiments/viewer-persistence-proof/`); the re-assert doubles as the SELF-HEAL for already-leaked machines (it migrates the viewer back out of IndexedDB, so it dies at that browser's next close). Shape pinned by `calendar-viewer-parity.test.mjs` Contract C — no unit or e2e test can see this property, because it only exists across a genuine browser exit
- `probeResetAtMs(email, password, memberName, timeoutMs)` (Oct 2026; replaced `getResetAtMs` in the same-day bug check) — the member's last admin-reset time, asked BEFORE the app signs in: a throwaway Identity Toolkit REST sign-in, then `passwordStatus/{name}` over Firestore REST. The SDK never sees the token, so nothing is announced to `onAuthStateChanged` or stored on the device until the check passes. Returns ms (0 = never reset), `null` (unconfirmed), or `'credential'` (password rejected — the ordinary SDK sign-in then fails and handles it), or `'throttled'` (v24.70 — Identity Toolkit's `TOO_MANY_ATTEMPTS`; `ensureFirebaseSession` holds and records `auth/too-many-requests`, never retrying into the lock-out). An unconfirmed answer is recorded as `auth/reset-check-unavailable`, a TRANSIENT code, so the page's retry path runs rather than leaving the Calendar locked until the next full load.
- `getPasswordStatus(memberName)` / `savePasswordSetAt(memberName)` / `getAllPasswordStatus()` / `reauthenticateWithPassword(memberName, typed)` / `setOwnPassword(memberName, newPassword)` / `resetMemberPassword(memberName, {revoke})` — `passwordStatus` collection + password change/reset (v18.63, PASSWORD_DESIGN.md Track C). `getPasswordStatus`/`savePasswordSetAt` are owner-scoped (Settings → Password); `getAllPasswordStatus` is the admin-only all-docs read for the Operations Account-status card; `reauthenticateWithPassword` re-authenticates the current user by trying `credentialCandidatesFor` (so a member on the surname default can confirm with their surname); `setOwnPassword` calls `updatePassword` then stamps `passwordSetAt` — the stamp is best-effort and returns `{ statusRecorded }`, NEVER rejecting on a stamp-only failure (the password already changed; rejecting would mis-report "password change failed" and strand the user on a now-invalid old password); `resetMemberPassword` POSTs to the admin-only `resetMemberPassword` Cloud Function with the caller's ID token (server resets the member to their surname default, optionally revokes their refresh tokens, and stamps `resetAt`)
- `withClaimRetry(fn)` (renamed from `writeWithClaimRetry` v17.08) — runs a Firestore thunk (READ or WRITE) and, on a stale-claim `permission-denied` with a live user, force-refreshes the ID token (`getIdToken(true)`) once and retries once (SECURITY_RELEASE_PLAN.md → B3 hardening, v15.18). A write thunk must rebuild its batch each call (a `WriteBatch` can't be re-committed). Used by `recordRangeOverrides`/`executeSave`/`_saveOverrideBatches`/`links-app.js` (writes) and by the Operations admin-gated read cards (`operations-app.js`, `operations-errors.js`, `operations-usage.js`, `operations-speed.js`; v17.08 — was the byte-identical local `adminReadWithRetry`). **Every request is wrapped in `abandonOnSignOut`** (v24.36, claim-retry.js), so it rejects with `SIGNED_OUT_CODE` once the signed-in account changes instead of waiting for good on a commit Firestore never settles.
- `writeWithClaimRetry(fn)` — no longer a plain alias (v24.39): `withClaimRetry` run through `runGatedWrite` (claim-retry.js), so once a write ends unconfirmed no new WRITE starts on the page until reload. Every write call site uses this name; reads keep `withClaimRetry` and are not gated.
- `getStaffContact(memberName)` / `saveStaffContact(memberName, workEmail)` / `deleteStaffContact(memberName)` / `getAllStaffContacts()` — `staffContact` collection; singular helpers called from `settings-app.js`; `getAllStaffContacts` called from `operations-app.js`
- **The nine error-log and analytics functions below are still exported here, and every importer is unchanged, but are bound from `buildAnalyticsClient` (`analytics-client.js`) since v24.64** — the Firestore I/O moved so it loads in Node; the descriptions below still say what each does.
- `logClientError(data)` / `getClientErrors()` / `resolveClientError(id)` — `clientErrors` collection (v13.31); `logClientError` called from `error-reporter.js`, read/resolve called from `operations-errors.js`. Ordering/retention policy delegated to `client-errors.js` (v13.48). `getClientErrors()` returns `{ errors, truncated }` — the index-free (no-`orderBy`) unresolved query fetches `CAP + 1` and `truncated` is true only when the extra row comes back (i.e. > 100 actually exist, not exactly 100), so the Error Log card shows a "showing the first 100" banner instead of silently hiding the rest (no-silent-caps; v15.69).
- `recordPageView(pageId)` / `recordActiveAccount({month, day})` / `getUsageStats()` — anonymous `analytics` usage counters (v14.14). `recordPageView`/`recordActiveAccount` are increment-only fire-and-forget, called from `usage-reporter.js`; `getUsageStats` reads THIS + LAST month page-view docs + the active-account doc (and prunes stale daily buckets) for the Operations Usage card (`operations-usage.js`) (page popularity has a This/Last-month toggle; accounts keep this-month + rolling-30). Date/aggregation maths delegated to `usage-stats.js` (incl. `prevMonthKey`). No member identity is stored — uniqueness is deduped client-side in `usage-reporter.js`, and admin (developer) loads are excluded at write time (v14.95).
- `fetchSignedDocumentUrl(kind)` (v24.19) — POSTs `{ kind }` to the `getDocumentUrl` Function with a (non-forced) ID token and returns the SHORT-LIVED url with its expiry and file type, **or `null`**. It never throws, because every caller has a working fallback — the permanent `storageUrl` already in the document — so there is nothing useful to do with an exception; `null` means "carry on the old way". Returns `{ url, expiresAt, fileType, storagePath }` (`document-url.js`'s `SignedDocumentUrl`; `storagePath` since v24.33) or `null`. Everything that reaches `null` is ordinary rather than exceptional: a **503** (only if the `serviceAccountTokenCreator` grant is missing — RECOVERY_RUNBOOK.md records it as granted), a **403** (a lapsed claim; the surfaces are already gated client-side), a **404** (nothing published yet), a timeout, or no `currentUser` at all, which returns early rather than spending a round trip to be refused. The token is deliberately NOT force-refreshed: this runs on every document open, and the server's `checkRevoked: true` is what actually catches a disabled account. The three call sites mint it BEFORE the click — awaiting inside a handler spends the user gesture, which pop-up-blocks `window.open` and drops the PWA out of standalone — except the nav drawer, which opened its tab on the gesture already and so has none left to lose.
- `getSignInStats()` (v18.96) — admin-only GET to the `getSignInStats` Cloud Function (fresh ID token as a Bearer header, like `resetMemberPassword`), returning `{ total, last7, last30, neverSignedIn }`. The EXACT unique-account counterpart to `getUsageStats`'s account figures, which are device-deduped and so count a two-device member twice. Exact for free: **Firebase Auth already stores one `lastSignInTime` per account**, so uniqueness is a property of the data and no new per-account record has to be written — which is why this route was chosen over giving the server a per-member activity doc (that would mean recording who opened the app and when, attendance-adjacent data in a workplace). Four integers come back; no identity. Counting/exclusion judgement lives in the pure, unit-tested `summariseSignIns` (`functions/roster-parse-helpers.js`): disabled leavers ignored entirely, admins excluded (mirroring `recordUsage`'s write-time filter), a missing `lastSignInTime` counted as never-signed-in rather than a stale sign-in, and a future timestamp treated as recent so clock skew can't erase a member.
- `getAccountSetupGaps()` (v22.53) — admin-only GET to the `getAccountSetupGaps` Cloud Function (same fresh-ID-token shape, 65s budget above the endpoint's own 60s ceiling). Returns `{ refused?, setUp: [{name, why}], leavers: [name] }`. **`refused` present is not the same as "no gaps"** and must never be rendered as one — the server could not see the roster's accounts and declined to answer.
- `recordPerfSample({page, metric, bucket, mode, conn})` — anonymous page-load latency counter (Project 0, v14.89), increment-only fire-and-forget into `analytics/perf_<YYYY-MM>.samples[<key>]` — **BATCHED since the Sep 2026 review**: samples accumulate in a `perf-stats.createPerfBatcher` and go as ONE merged `setDoc` (several `increment(n)` fields) 1s after the first sample, then at idle, or at once on `pagehide`/hidden; the rules' anti-wipe `hasAll` guard accepts it unchanged. Called from `perf-reporter.js`; the key is built by `perf-stats.perfSampleKey` from `APP_VERSION` + the dimensions (all non-identifying — no member, no raw ms).
- `getPerfStats()` — admin-only read for the Operations "App speed" card (v14.90; two journeys v14.92; FCP + month-over-month v14.95): reads THIS + LAST month `analytics/perf_<YYYY-MM>` and returns `{ thisMonth, lastMonth }`, each `{ month, login, fcp, pages, ready }` — plain-language `summarisePerf` summaries for the `loginTotal`, `fcp`, `domReady` and (v20.80) `ready` metrics. The card render (`initPageSpeedCard` in `operations-speed.js`) has a This/Last-month toggle and shows "🔑 Signing in" + "📄 Opening pages" as THREE milestones in timeline order — ✨ First appears = fcp, 📦 Code loaded = domReady, ✅ Usable = ready — with three bars per page, then "🔍 A closer look" at the busiest page (the raw `samples` map is carried through for it). The third is the one an admin should read as "how fast is the app"; the two before it are stages of getting there and neither is the answer. `ready` is a smaller population by design (only pages that mark the milestone report it) and the card says so rather than hiding the bar; bucketing/verdict maths is the pure `perf-stats.js` module.
- `uploadCircular(date, file, uploadedBy)` — writes the file (PDF or Word .docx) to `circulars/{date}-{uploadId}.{ext}` in Firebase Storage (versioned path; old file deleted after Firestore commit succeeds) and upserts the `circulars/{date}` Firestore doc (includes `storagePath` field for cleanup tracking); also fire-and-forget prunes documents older than 6 months via `pruneOldDocs()` (`doc-retention.js`) after each upload; called from `operations-app.js` (v13.58, versioned path v13.99)
- `getLatestCircular()` — queries `circulars` collection, returns latest doc's data (with `storageUrl`) or null; called from `nav-panel.js` (☰ direct open) and `calendar-doc-viewer.js` (notification-tap viewer) (v13.58)
- `uploadNewsletter(date, file, uploadedBy)` — writes the file (PDF or Word .docx) to `newsletters/{date}-{uploadId}.{ext}` in Firebase Storage (versioned path; old file deleted after Firestore commit succeeds) and upserts the `newsletters/{date}` Firestore doc (includes `storagePath` field for cleanup tracking); also fire-and-forget prunes documents older than 6 months via `pruneOldDocs()` (`doc-retention.js`) after each upload; called from `operations-app.js` (v13.59, versioned path v13.99)
- `getLatestNewsletter()` — queries `newsletters` collection, returns latest doc's data (with `storageUrl`) or null; called from `nav-panel.js` (☰ direct open) and `calendar-doc-viewer.js` (notification-tap viewer) (v13.59)

### `fetch-timeout.js`

The bound on every call the app makes to a Cloud Function (v20.52, external review). Exports
`serverRefusal(err)` (whole-app review, Oct 2026) — the server's 4xx refusal inside a caller's `Server responded <status>: <body>` error, with the JSON `error` sentence as the reason, or null for a 5xx, a network failure or a timeout; Operations' Reset shows it and offers no Retry, since retrying cannot change an answer. `fetchWithTimeout(url, options, timeoutMs)`, `isFetchTimeout(err)`, `FETCH_TIMEOUT_CODE`, `isFetchAborted(err)` / `FETCH_ABORTED_CODE` — the last pair distinguishes OUR timeout from somebody ELSE'S abort (a navigation, a caller's own controller), because getting that backwards is what tells an admin a write "may still have gone through" after a request that never left — and
`DEFAULT_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS`.

**The deadline covers the BODY (Sep 2026 review).** `fetch` resolves on headers; the bound used to be
cleared there, so `r.json()` on a body that stalled afterwards waited for ever. The returned
Response's `json`/`text`/`arrayBuffer`/`blob`/`formData` now settle within the same deadline and
reject with `FETCH_TIMEOUT_CODE` when it cuts them off; the three write callers (reset password,
reset request, Set up accounts) read the body inside their timeout `try`, so that case gets the
"may still have gone through" copy. The Calendar's PIN exchange moved onto the helper for the same
reason.

Five client calls reached Functions through a bare `fetch()` with no bound. A stalled transport
never rejects, so the awaiting UI simply stops — the login card's reset-request button on
"Sending…" for ever, "Set up accounts" never reporting. That is not a wrong answer, it is the
absence of one, which is worse: the member cannot tell a slow network from a broken app.

**Two rules, both easy to get backwards:**

- **A budget sits ABOVE the endpoint's own `timeoutSeconds`, never below.** A client that gives up
  first turns a slow-but-succeeding operation into a reported failure — for `setupRosterAuth` that
  means telling an admin a change did not happen while the server is still making it happen. Hence
  35s/65s/130s against 30s/60s/120s ceilings. The bound exists to end an INFINITE wait, not to make
  a slow call feel fast.
- **A timeout on a STATE-CHANGING call does not mean it did not happen.** `AbortController` stops us
  waiting; it does not stop the server working. So the write call sites say "may still have gone
  through — go and check", never "failed". Same distinction `password-force.js` draws between
  `withTimeout` and `settleOrTimeout`. Reads may say "failed" freely.

`isFetchTimeout` exists because a foreign abort (a navigation, a caller's own signal) arrives as the
same `AbortError` our bound produces; only the internal `timedOut` flag separates them, and the
callers' copy depends on that separation. `calendar-access.js` still keeps its own v20.45 bound;
the PIN rollout it was waiting on closed 26 Aug 2026, so moving it onto this is open work.

### `error-reporter.js`
Shared uncaught-error reporter (v13.31). Only export is `initErrorReporter()` — installs `window.onerror` + `unhandledrejection` listeners that write capped, deduped records to the Firestore `clientErrors` collection via `logClientError` (fire-and-forget; never throws, never blocks). **Requires an auth context** — the three canonical call patterns (calendar after `calendarAuthReady`; `sessionReady.then(...)` on the five session pages — admin, settings, operations, links, overtime; paycalc's `afterAuth`) are pinned in CLAUDE.md → `initErrorReporter()` call pattern. Records surface on the Operations → Error Log card.

### `usage-reporter.js`
Anonymous usage recorder (v14.14) — the usage analogue of `error-reporter.js`. `recordUsage(page, signedIn?, selected?)` (two identity arguments again since v25.00 — see the last bold paragraph): records an anonymous page-view counter, and — whenever the acting member can be identified — counts that account toward the active-account metric, deduped client-side via localStorage flags keyed by member name (`myb_usage_m_*`, `myb_usage_d30_*`) so the server only ever receives `increment(1)` and never learns who was active. **Records nothing when `identity` is in `CONFIG.ADMIN_NAMES`** — the developer's own test loads are excluded so figures reflect real staff (v14.95). **ONE identity argument since v19.95, and ANONYMOUS VISITS NOW COUNT.** There were two: `member` (the signed-in name) gated the account metric, so the calendar — which has no Auth session — passed `member: null` and a member who only ever reads the roster incremented the page counter and nothing else. That is most of the staff (`password-force.js` reaches only people who sign in; AUTH_PLAN.md Track E's whole premise is that a roster-only viewer never does), so "Accounts active" counted the minority who open an authenticated page while reading as though it counted everybody. `_recordOrigin` had already worked around precisely this by keying on `identity` (v19.23) — its comment says so — and rather than leave one metric compensating for the gate above it, the gate keys on `identity` too and the parameters collapse. Consequences to know: the figures **step up at v19.95 and are not comparable either side of it**; the dedup keys are shared across the calendar and authenticated routes on purpose (one member reading the roster and then opening Settings is ONE account); and a FIRST-RUN device still records only a page view, because there is no identity to dedup on and the "selection" at that moment is the default member, an admin. **ONLY A SIGNED-IN ADMIN IS LEFT OUT, AND A RELEASE RELOAD IS NOT A VISIT (v25.00, Usage card review).** The one identity argument also did the admin exclusion, so on a device with nobody signed in it excluded on the SELECTED member — and the station PC reads the roster on the staff PIN with whichever row was picked last, the default being an admin. A selection left there dropped every staff open, while the admin's own PIN opens counted whenever another row was picked. Now `signedIn` alone can exclude; `selected` (the Calendar's, null on first run) is only a dedup key; and an admin SELECTION records the page view but no member-device and no address, because there is no member to name. A load the app reloaded itself for an update (`perf-reporter.loadFollowedRelease`, the same once-per-load answer App Speed reads) records nothing — about one Calendar open in seven in September. The rolling counts MOVE rather than freeze (`usage-stats.rollingMove`): each open on a new day takes the account-device's +1 off its old day (inside the window) and puts it on today, in one merged write, so "last 30 days" counts each account-device on its LATEST active day. Called once per page from each coordinator at the same point as `initErrorReporter()`. Imports the I/O from `firebase-client.js`, the dedup maths from `usage-stats.js`, `lsGet`/`lsSet` from `ls.js`, `CONFIG` from `roster-data.js` and `loadFollowedRelease` from `perf-reporter.js`. Fire-and-forget — never throws.

- `recordOpen(itemId, signedIn?)` (v18.20) — anonymous "opened" counter for documents/guides, sharing the pv_ counts map and the admin exclusion; ids `huddle`/`circular`/`newsletter`/`guide-staff`/`guide-paycalc`/`guide-railcard`/`guide-fip`/`guide-rangers` (allowlisted in firestore.rules). **Every guide has been counted since v19.95**, and the Rangers guide arrived at v20.05 already carrying one — the Staff & Admin Guide and the Pay Calculator Guide had no counter before that, so the "Documents & guides" group answered "which of the two REFERENCE guides is read more" while reading as "which guides are read". The id lives on the `NAV_GUIDES` entry and is stamped onto the link as `data-open-id`, never matched from the href: `'./paycalc-guide.html'.includes('guide.html')` is TRUE, and `guide.html` was the Staff Guide's own filename until the v22.47 rename — so a substring test counted every Pay Calculator Guide open as a Staff Guide open with both bars still looking plausible. The rename ended that collision; the id stays explicit because a filename is not a stable key. `firestore-contract-parity.test.mjs` fails if an entry has no `openId` or the list drifts from `OPEN_META`. No dedup — every open counts. Called from `nav-panel.js` (drawer doc opens + guide-link taps), `calendar-huddle-viewer.js` (viewer auto-open) and `calendar-doc-viewer.js` (notification-tap Open button) — **every caller passes the SIGNED-IN name, never the Calendar's selected member** (v25.00: on the station PC a selection left on the admin's row dropped every staff open there; the drawer's old `usageIdentity` option went with it). Rendered by the Usage card's "Documents & guides — opens" group.

### `usage-stats.js`
Pure date-bucketing + aggregation for the usage analytics — no DOM, no Firebase. Imported by `firebase-client.js` and `usage-reporter.js`; tested by `usage-stats.test.mjs`.
- `monthKey(d)` / `dayKey(d)` — "YYYY-MM" / "YYYY-MM-DD" local-time keys
- `shouldCountMonth(lastMonth, now)` — the per-calendar-month dedup decision
- `rollingMove(lastMs, now, [windowDays])` → `{ add, remove }` or null (v25.00; replaced `shouldCountRolling`) — where an account-device's ONE rolling-window count should sit after an open: today, taken back from its old day while that day is still inside the window. The frozen-for-30-days rule it replaced summed the days accounts were first seen, so anyone whose +1 had aged out while they kept using the app was missed (simulated: ~1 in 30 daily users, ~1 in 6 weekly, ~1 in 5 monthly). Null when today already holds it, so at most one write per account-device per day; the device flag's meaning is unchanged, so flags written by the old code migrate as they are.
- `recentDayKeys(now, [days])` / `sumDailyWindow(daily, now, [days])` — the rolling-window day keys and their summed counts ("active in last 30 days"), never below zero (a lost +1 can leave a moved count's -1 behind until its day leaves the window)
- `prevMonthKey(d)` — "YYYY-MM" for the calendar month before `d` (year-boundary safe); the "last month" comparison window for the Operations cards
- `orderPageCounts(counts)` — page-view counts → `[{page, count}]` sorted desc then page-name asc
- `staleDailyKeys(daily, now, [keepDays])` — daily buckets outside the retention window, for pruning
- Constants `ROLLING_WINDOW_DAYS` (30) and `DAILY_RETENTION_DAYS` — the default rolling-window and daily-bucket retention sizes the functions above fall back to
- **Per-ADDRESS migration counters (v19.23)** — the app is served from two addresses during the move off the GitHub Pages mirror, and nothing recorded which one anyone was using:
  - `originLabel(hostname)` → `'web' | 'pages' | 'fb' | 'other'`. A short STABLE LABEL, never the raw hostname: a `.` in a Firestore map key is a field-path hazard (the same trap `perfSampleKey` sanitises for), a closed set keeps the key space bounded, and the label survives a domain change.
  - `originKey(day, origin, [installed])` / `parseOriginKey(key)` — `YYYY-MM-DD|<origin>[|pwa]`. `parseOriginKey` returns **null** for anything unparseable, so a stray key can never be counted as a date.
  - `summariseOrigins(daily, now, [days])` → `[{origin, accounts, installed}]`, busiest first. `accounts` is an account-DEVICE signal, not a headcount — each account-device holds one +1 per address, on its latest active day (`rollingMove`); `installed` is the same rule for opens of the installed app, so it is a subset of the same accounts, not a second population. Rows are clamped (never negative, `installed` never above `accounts`) and an empty one is dropped.
  - `staleOriginKeys(daily, now, [keepDays])` — retention prune. Also returns keys that do NOT parse: `summariseOrigins` skips exactly those, so without this a malformed writer could grow the doc for ever with nothing to remove it.

### `perf-reporter.js`
Anonymous page-load latency recorder (Project 0 instrumentation, v14.89) — the performance analogue of `usage-reporter.js`. `recordPageLatency(page, identity?)` records, per page load, the six metrics the App Speed card reads and nothing else: `fcp` (first contentful paint — on the Calendar, the splash), `domReady` (the app's code loaded), `ready` (the page usable, from `markPageReady`), `readyUpdate` (the same moment on a load a release caused), `rosterSnapshot` (the Calendar's stored copy painted before the sign-in check) and `loginTotal` (pressing Sign in → usable, page id `'login'`). Each goes through `firebase-client.recordPerfSample` as an anonymous bucketed counter with PWA mode and connection class. **When `identity` is an admin (the developer), nothing is recorded** — but both one-shot markers (sign-in, release) are still consumed so neither can mis-time a later load. Called once per page from each coordinator beside `recordUsage()`; the Calendar passes the SIGNED-IN member (`getSession()`), never the selected name, so a station PC left on an admin's roster does not drop every staff open on it. Fire-and-forget — never throws, never blocks. **No identity and no raw millisecond value is ever stored.**
- **A SAMPLE IS TIME SOMEBODY SPENT WAITING (App Speed review, Oct 2026).** Two kinds of time used to reach the figures that were not the app being slow, and both are now excluded at the source:
  - **Loads nobody watched.** A reading counts only if the page had been visible from the start of the load up to it (`_watched`): a load that STARTS hidden — the Calendar's release reload, which runs while the page is hidden by design, about one Calendar open in seven in September — records none of the ordinary metrics, and a Chrome prerender is excluded by the same rule. A hide before the module ran is read back from the `visibility-state` timeline where the browser keeps one.
  - **Time spent typing.** `markAwaitingInput()` — called by `login-overlay.js` when its form mounts and, through the lock cards' injected `onAwaitingInput`, when a Calendar PIN or member card appears — marks the moment the page started waiting for a person. `ready` and `rosterSnapshot` reached after it are dropped; `fcp` and `domReady` cannot include typing and are kept. A sign-in has its own figure.
- **`readyUpdate` times the WAIT a person had.** A reload watched from its start is timed from the start like any open, and written beside `ready` from the same reading. A reload that started hidden is timed from the member COMING BACK to the page until it was usable (a token 1 ms when it was ready first), and dropped if they leave again before it is ready or have to type. The release marker is compared with when THIS load started (10 s window, 1 s of clock skew), never with when the reading runs — a hidden reload can sit frozen for minutes, and the old "within a minute of now" check dropped the label from exactly those loads. The marker is written by `sw-register.js` at each reload (`reloadForUpdate` and its siblings), so a declined reload labels nothing.
- `markPageReady()` — the `ready` mark, stamped after the frame that shows the page (`requestAnimationFrame` then `setTimeout`) on a visible page, at once on a hidden one; the first call wins. A page that never marks records no `ready` — absent, never borrowed from `domReady`, which is when the SCRIPTS finish (measured with the auth restore held at 2s: fcp 512 ms, domReady 669 ms, roster on screen 2630 ms). The reading waits on a module-scoped promise, so a mark that arrives after `recordPageLatency` ran is still recorded (v21.16 — reading the mark table once sampled the fastest fifth of loads).
- `PAGE_READY_MARK` — the mark's name, shared by producer and consumer.
- `loadFollowedRelease()` (v25.00) — did a release cause THIS load? Reads and consumes `sw-register.js`'s one-shot marker once per load and remembers the answer, because two readers need it: `recordPageLatency` (the `readyUpdate` label) and `usage-reporter.recordUsage`, which does not count such a load as a page view. `recordUsage` runs first on every page, so an unshared read would have taken the marker from App Speed.
- `markMilestone('rosterSnapshot')` — the one milestone left; `calendar-app.js`'s `_paintSnapshotBeforeGrant` marks it when the member's own stored roster (calendar-snapshot.js) is actually painted, so an open with no copy gives it no time.
- `markLoginStart()` / `clearLoginStart()` — the sign-in marker in `sessionStorage['myb_perf_login_t0']`; it survives the post-login reload, is read once, and is cleared on a failed sign-in or a sign-in that leads to no page (Overtime without access, the Pay Calculator for an unsupported role), so a later load cannot count that time. `loginTotal` ends at the `ready` mark — it ended whenever `recordPageLatency` ran until Oct 2026, before the Calendar's grid — and is recorded only when the page was watched until then.
- **Retired with the card blocks that read them (Oct 2026), writes and all:** `ttfb`, the three boot phases (`swBoot`/`sdkLoad`/`appBoot` and the `myb-sdk-ready` mark), the start ladder's other rungs (`authBoot`, `access`, `rosterCached`, `rosterLive`), the saved-copy/server split (`readyCached`/`readyFetched` — `markPageReady` no longer takes a source), and the service worker's fetch count (`swrCount`, `readyHeavySwr`, the `REVALIDATION_COUNT` message). They answered the sign-in speed investigation `LATENCY.md` closed on 19 September; what they found is recorded there.
- Tested by `perf-reporter.test.mjs`, which drives the real recorder with pages that hide, load hidden, prerender or wait for typing, and asserts ABSENCE.

### `perf-stats.js`
Pure latency maths for the perf pipeline — no DOM, no Firebase, no timing reads. Imported by `analytics-client.js`, `perf-reporter.js` and `operations-speed.js`; tested by `perf-stats.test.mjs`.
- `PERF_BUCKETS` — the five coarse, map-key-safe duration buckets (`lt500ms` … `over8s`)
- `createPerfBatcher(write, arm)` — coalesces samples into ONE merged write per month (`add(month, key)` / `flush()`); the scheduling is injected (`arm` receives the flush), so it holds counts and no timers. Used by `recordPerfSample` (review F5).
- `bucketDuration(ms)` — ms → a bucket id, or `null` for a non-finite/negative value (skip the sample)
- `perfSampleKey({version,page,metric,bucket,mode,conn})` / `parsePerfSampleKey(key)` — the pipe-joined Firestore map key and its inverse (sanitises the version's dots → `_`, since a `.` is a Firestore field-path hazard in a map key)
- `SPEED_GROUPS` — the three plain-language bands (`quick`/`ok`/`slow`) the buckets roll up into, with labels + tone
- **A stored count must be a count.** The sample values are written by any signed-in session and the rules cannot check them, so every reader takes a value only when it is a positive whole number under a million (`_count`): `Number()` read `true` as 1, `"7"` as 7 and 1e308 as a count, and two of the last printed "∞ page opens".
- **The three percentages always add to 100** (largest remainder, ties to the faster band) — rounding each band alone printed 34 + 34 + 33.
- `summarisePerf(samples, {metric})` — overall + per-page band counts and percentages for one metric.
- `summarisePerfBy(samples, {page, metric, dimension, minSamples, splitAt})` / `PERF_DIMENSIONS` (v20.19) — one page's samples split by `mode` / `version` (or `conn`, which the card no longer shows). Versions ROLL UP newest-first into ranges big enough to read (`minSamples`), the oldest thin remainder merging into its neighbour. **No version row crosses `splitAt`** (Oct 2026): from `CLEAN_TIMINGS_FROM` the timings are taken a different way, so a bucket closes at the split even when thin, the remainder never merges across it, and each row carries `afterSplit`.
- `perfVerdict(overall, kind?)` — a plain-English verdict + tone, `kind` `'pages'|'login'|'fcp'|'ready'`. ≥20% slow → bad; ≥80% quick AND under 10% slow → good (Oct 2026 — 81/0/19 was green one point from 80/0/20 being red); otherwise amber with one of three sentences: `ok` ("mostly quick") when quick is the larger band AND at least half (v24.96), `mixed` ("under half … are quick") when it is the larger band but under half (Oct 2026 — those fell to "most often take a moment", so 124/100/26 and 50/40/10 showed the same 50 · 40 · 10 under two sentences, the first false), `moment`/`momentSlow` when "a moment" is at least as common. `none` and `thin` name no month, because the headline renders under Last month too.
- **`THIN_SAMPLE` (20) is the whole card's threshold** (v21.16) — the rows' "(few)", each By-page bar's own "(few)", and the headline verdicts, which short-circuit to `tone: 'thin'` below it: the percentage is stated, the CLAIM is withheld.
- `compactPct(pct, count, total)` / `shareLabel(part, whole)` (Oct 2026) — a share never rounds to a lie: a real share under 1% reads "<1%" / "under 1%", one short of the whole ">99%" / "over 99%"; `shareLabel` refuses a part larger than its whole on the RAW counts (251 of 250 rounded to "100%" under the old check).
- `EARLY_ROSTER` / `summariseEarlyRoster` / `EARLY_ROSTER_FROM` ('24.85') / `earlyRosterShareLabel(samples, {page})` — the stored-copy paint and how often it had the chance: `rosterSnapshot` over `ready`, both only on versions that can record a copy (it divided by every version's `ready` until v24.89, printing 19% for a true 60%), and only from `THIN_SAMPLE` opens.
- `UPDATE_OPENS` / `summariseUpdateOpens` / `UPDATE_OPENS_FROM` ('24.98') — the wait after a release, read only from the version that times it as a wait (older versions timed the background load).
- `CLEAN_TIMINGS_FROM` ('24.98') / `shareMeasuredBefore(samples, from?)` — the share of a month's code-loaded opens timed by versions before the measurement change, so the card can say a month mixes the two ways of timing rather than present the change as the app getting faster. Both version constants MUST name the release that ships the recorder change.
