  `writeWithClaimRetry` (Resolve is a gated write) and `appVersion` (the speed-sample key).
  `firebase-client.js` builds it once and re-exports each function under its old name.

**Two properties, in its header:** no identity in a counter, and telemetry never affects the app —
every writer swallows its rejection and every prune is wrapped so it cannot reject the read it rides
on (the prunes use `FieldPath`; a dotted `daily.<date>` path throws synchronously). Executed against a
fake Firestore by `analytics-client.test.mjs`.

### `documents-client.js`
The three date-keyed document collections — Daily Huddle, Weekly Retail Circular, Marylebone
Newsletter — and the upload sequence behind them (extracted from `firebase-client.js`, v21.90).
- `buildDocumentClient(deps)` → `{ uploadHuddle, uploadCircular, uploadNewsletter, getLatestCircular,
  getLatestNewsletter, subscribeToLatestHuddle }`. `deps` carries `db`, `collections`, the Firestore
  functions (`fs`), `getStorageSdk`, `uploadBytesWithClaimRetry`, the pure path/type `utils`,
  `resolveUploadCommit`, `pruneOldDocs` and (v24.39) `guardCommit` — `abandonOnSignOut` from firebase-client.js, identity by default; a commit abandoned on sign-out throws `upload/unconfirmed`, never a failure and never a rollback.
- `assertFileSignature(file, expectedType)` — magic-number check; fails CLOSED on an unreadable or
  too-short file. Exported separately because it is a decision, not a step.

**One invariant: a live document must never point at a file that is not there.** An upload writes to
two systems with no transaction across them, so every rule here exists to keep them in step across a
failure — check the signature before anything is written; upload to a VERSIONED path so the old file
stays readable until Firestore commits; roll the new object back only on a DEFINITE non-commit,
because a still-retriable final error is commit-ambiguous and deleting there would 404 a committed
document; and resolve that ambiguity by READING rather than re-issuing blind, since a blind retry can
overwrite a competing upload's file reference with a path that upload has already deleted.

**Why a factory rather than a module that imports `db`.** `firebase-client.js` re-exports these, so
importing back would be a cycle `import-graph.test.mjs` refuses. The reason that earns it, though, is
the second one: with every Firebase handle injected the module imports nothing from gstatic and loads
in Node — so the orderings above can be forced against fakes, which no arrangement of real calls can
do. `doc-retention.js` gained exactly this at v21.86. The pure decisions it composes live in
`upload-commit.js` and `doc-retention.js`; this module owns the SEQUENCE, not the rules.
- **The retry is a COMPARE-AND-SET (v22.19, external review).** The reconciliation read closed the first TOCTOU (a `deadline-exceeded` can be raised after the server committed, so the retry reads before re-issuing) — but the read and the write are two calls, and a competing upload can commit between them: the same race one step later. A blind second `setDoc` there overwrites their file reference with a Storage path their upload has already deleted, so the live document 404s behind the staff button and nothing errors. The retry now re-reads inside `runTransaction` and writes only if the document is still what the check was made against, asking the SAME `resolveUploadCommit` rather than restating it. Its test harness had to gain a real transaction to see any of this: `runTransaction: async () => {}` never called its callback, so the CAS write silently stopped happening.

### `firestore.rules`
Server-side Firestore security rules — deployed via `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules`.
- `overrides` create/update: **per-member isolation (B3 strict 3-tier, shipped v16.29; was B2 permissive, v14.53)** — `memberName() == request.resource.data.memberName || token.admin == true || token.manager == true` (and on update the EXISTING doc's `memberName` must pass the same test). `memberName()` is the token's `name` only when `isMember()` holds — a password-provider sign-in whose `email` is the one derived from that name and whose server-set `member` claim matches it — and null otherwise. The legacy/anonymous `!('name' in token)` no-name escape branch has been **removed** in B3, so a token with no `name` claim can no longer write; stale tokens self-heal via `writeWithClaimRetry` (`CONFIG.CLAIM_EPOCH`, currently 3). Required fields: `date`, `memberName`, `type`, `value`, `note`, `source`, `createdAt` (a timestamp); `date` bounded to a real `YYYY-MM-DD` (month 01-12, day 01-31); `source` must be `'manual'` or `'roster_import'`; type↔value consistency enforced (timed types `shift`/`rdw` require `HH:MM-HH:MM`; `spare_shift` → `'SPARE'`; `annual_leave` → `'AL'`; `correction` → `'RD'`; `sick` → `'SICK'`). History (the v10.72→v10.94 outage): KNOWN_LIMITATIONS.md task #2.
- `overrides` read: `isMember()`, `admin`, or `calendarViewer` on a `custom`-provider (PIN-minted) sign-in (v20.12 — anonymous is refused).
- `overrides` delete: same **strict 3-tier** check (no no-name escape, v16.29) against the existing doc's `memberName` (was any authenticated user before v14.53).
- Custom claims set by `setupRosterAuth`: `{ admin: true, name }` for the admin list (`CONFIG.ADMIN_NAMES = ['G. Miller']`), `{ manager: true, name }` for the manager list (`CONFIG.MANAGER_NAMES`), `{ name }` for everyone else. **B4 (v16.30): these lists are SERVER-OWNED** — read from `roster-members.json` (generated from `roster-data.js`), NOT the client payload, so a tampered request can't self-promote a tier (admin outranks manager; `setCustomUserClaims` replaces all claims so a demoted member loses the elevated claim on the next run). The admin bypass is essential for roster upload (writes overrides for all members); the manager bypass lets managers edit staff data on behalf without master-admin powers.
- `huddles` / `circulars` / `newsletters` read: **`isMember()`, `admin`, or `calendarViewer` on a `custom`-provider (PIN-minted) sign-in** (v23.18, owner decision 7 Sep 2026 — mirrors `overrides`; the client stopped reading without access at v23.17, `calendar-doc-access.js`). It was `allow read;` from v10.76, when requiring auth broke notification auto-open on fresh visits — the gate answers that differently now: the tap is HELD and finished by the PIN.
- `huddles` write (Firestore): requires auth + `admin == true`; `hasOnly` enforces no extra fields; `uploadedAt` must be a timestamp; optional `htmlContent` capped at 250 000 chars (v10.83+).
- `staffContact` read: owner (`request.auth.token.name == memberName`, where `memberName` is the document ID) or admin; write: owner or admin, and requires the `name` JWT claim (set by `setupRosterAuth`) so anonymous fallback sessions cannot write (v12.68).
- `pushSubscriptions` read: `if false`. Create/update: requires auth (`request.auth != null`), refuses a `calendarViewer` token (the shared PIN uid is the same on every office PC), + `hasOnly(['endpoint','keys','subscribedAt','owner'])`; the optional `owner` (Firebase Auth uid), when present, must equal `request.auth.uid`, and an update must also match the EXISTING doc's owner when it has one. Delete (also refused to `calendarViewer`): **per-owner (A5 / F-SEC-5, v16.29→v17.76 hardening)** — allowed only when `resource.data.owner == request.auth.uid`, OR the doc carries no `owner` (legacy pre-owner docs, kept deletable so VAPID-rotation cleanup isn't locked out). Merely knowing a doc id no longer permits deletion.
- `clientErrors` write: any authenticated session; read/update/delete: admin only; shape-validated (v13.31).
- `circulars` / `newsletters` write: admin only (v13.58/v13.59), `hasOnly(['date','storageUrl','storagePath','fileType','uploadedAt','uploadedBy'])`, and `storagePath` must sit under that collection's own prefix. Read: see the `huddles` bullet above.
- `linkDesigns` read: `isMember()` or `admin` (not merely any authenticated session — an anonymous or PIN session is refused). Write: **`token.linksDesigner == true || token.admin == true`** (H2, shipped v16.29), and create/update are **shape-validated** (v17.02, Finding #12): `hasOnly(['name','patterns','window','revision','updatedAt','updatedBy','deletedAt','deletedBy'])` + `name` string 1–100, `patterns` map, `updatedAt` timestamp, `updatedBy` string ≤100, and the optional fields type-checked. Delete is allowed only on a document that already carries `deletedAt` (it is in the bin). The `linksDesigner` claim is set by `setupRosterAuth` from `CONFIG.LINKS_DESIGNERS`; `links-app.js` wraps `linkDesigns` writes in `writeWithClaimRetry` (stale-token self-heal) and saves via an atomic `runTransaction` (v17.02, Finding #13 — closes the co-designer check-then-act clobber; falls back to a queued setDoc offline). Before v16.29 any authenticated identity could write any Links design.

### `storage.rules`
Firebase Storage security rules.
- `huddles/{fileName}` read: admin only. The app never reads these files through the rules — it opens them through a short-lived signed URL (`getDocumentUrl`) or the stored tokenised download URL, both of which bypass them.
- `huddles/{fileName}` write: requires auth + `admin == true` + size < 20 MB + MIME type PDF or DOCX (v10.83). Cloud Function (ingestHuddle) uses Admin SDK — bypasses rules, unaffected. This rule is essential for the manual admin upload path in `huddle.js`.
- `circulars/{fileName}` / `newsletters/{fileName}` read: admin only, same as huddles — actual file access is via the signed or tokenised download URL, which bypasses these rules. Write: requires auth + `admin == true` + size ≤ 20 MB + MIME type PDF or DOCX (v13.58/v13.59; Word .docx allowed since v16.31).
- All other paths: denied.

### `index.css` / `admin.css` / `paycalc.css` / `operations.css` / `settings.css` / `links.css`
Page-specific CSS for each page — extracted from inline `<style>` blocks (index/admin/paycalc at v11.41; operations/settings at v12.01; links arrived with the page at v12.07).
- Edit here for any visual change that is specific to one page
- All are stale-while-revalidate in the service worker (v16.10 — same strategy and freshness lifecycle as their HTML and the app JS)

### `guide-shell.css`
Shared chrome for the five guide pages (`staff-guide.html`, `paycalc-guide.html`, `railcard-guide.html`, `fip-guide.html`, `rangers-guide.html`) — added v11.48.
- Holds common header/back-button/PDF-button/print rules and defines shared brand palette tokens (`--navy`, `--navy-dark`, `--navy-mid`, `--gold`) in its own `:root` (v11.85) — guide pages no longer define these themselves
- This is the one place to change guide chrome — do not re-inline it into the pages
- NOT the app's `shared.css` (which the guides deliberately don't import) — see `.claude/rules/guide-pages.md`

### `guide-doc.css`
Shared styles for the two document-style guides (`staff-guide.html`, `paycalc-guide.html`) — loaded between `guide-shell.css` and page CSS in those two pages. NOT linked by `railcard-guide.html`, `fip-guide.html` or `rangers-guide.html`.
- Two-column print layout, info/warning/tip boxes, numbered steps, data tables, callout banners
- Changes here affect both the Staff & Admin Guide and the Pay Calculator Guide simultaneously; check both in print preview before committing

### `staff-guide.css` / `paycalc-guide.css` / `railcard-guide.css` / `fip-guide.css` / `rangers-guide.css`
Page-specific CSS for each guide page — extracted from inline `<style>` blocks at v12.04 (`rangers-guide.css` arrived with its page).
- Edit the corresponding file for any visual change specific to that guide page
- All five are stale-while-revalidate in the service worker (v16.10 — same strategy and freshness lifecycle as their HTML and the app JS)
- Linked after `guide-shell.css` in each guide's `<head>`

### `purify.es.mjs`
Self-hosted DOMPurify ES module (v3.4.17) — extracted from CDN at v12.04.
- Imported by `calendar-huddle-viewer.js` for Huddle HTML sanitisation
- To upgrade: `npm pack dompurify@<ver>`, extract `package/dist/purify.es.mjs`, replace this file, RE-ADD the `// @ts-nocheck` first line (tsc follows the dynamic import), update version comment in `calendar-huddle-viewer.js`
- Precached by the service worker (stale-while-revalidate, like all app JS)

### `shared.css`
All CSS shared across all nine app pages (index, admin, paycalc, operations, settings, links, overtime, trains, newlink).
- CSS custom properties (`--primary-blue`, `--accent-gold`, etc.) — **never hardcode hex**
- Typography scale, badge/pill variants, button types
- `touch-only` class — hidden by default, revealed on `@media (pointer: coarse)` (touch devices)
- `@media print` rules — every shift type needs a print rule
- `.app-header` (v10.66): `display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1fr auto 1fr` — true centring regardless of burger/badge width asymmetry. Used by `admin.html` and `paycalc.html` (calendar uses a different `.header`).
- `.app-header-brand` (v10.66): flex wrapper (`display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 10px; justify-content: center`) holding the icon `<img>` and `<h1>`. Lives in the `auto` centre column.
- `.btn-burger` (v10.68): `justify-self: start` — pins burger to the left edge of its `1fr` column in the grid context. Ignored in flex contexts (e.g. the calendar header).
- `.huddle-open-btn` / `.huddle-open-prompt` (v10.71): styles for the in-overlay "📄 Open Huddle" button shown when a notification tap opens a PDF huddle. Defined in `index.css` (not shared.css — huddle viewer is index.html-only).

### `service-worker.js`
- Add any new JS/CSS/HTML file to both `NETWORK_FIRST_FILES` and `CORE_ASSETS`
- **A document cache-miss races the network against the cache only when there IS a cached fallback** (v24.61, iOS audit B3). `findCachedFallback()` (split out of `serveFallback`) answers first; with nothing cached — a first install, or iOS having evicted Cache Storage — the branch waits for the network however long it takes, because the 2s race served the synthesised "Offline" page to an ONLINE member on a slow connection and the abort killed the real fetch. That synthesised page (`offlinePage()`) now carries a "Try again" link to the request's own path — a plain link, since a synthesised response has no CSP and an installed app has no address bar.
- **`pushsubscriptionchange`** (v24.61, iOS audit B4) — when the push service rotates the endpoint, re-subscribe with the key the OLD subscription carried (`oldSubscription.options.applicationServerKey`, so this file holds no copy of the VAPID key) or take the ready-made `newSubscription`; the server record is re-saved by `notif.js`, which remembers the endpoint it last saved and bypasses its 24-hour throttle when the live one differs. Until then a rotation left a dead endpoint on the server and the bell silently reading off.
- Version string must match `APP_VERSION` in `roster-data.js`
- Strategies (v16.10): HTML + JS/CSS stale-while-revalidate from the version-pinned cache (JS/CSS revalidate once per SW lifetime; HTML revalidates via the navigation-preload response; HTML cache-miss → network-first with the 2s cache-fallback race); gstatic Firebase SDK cache-first from `myb-roster-sdk-v{FIREBASE_SDK_VERSION}` (constant must match firebase-client.js — enforced by sw-asset-check.test.mjs); icons/fonts/manifest cache-first
- Update lifecycle: install = skipWaiting only; activate = claim + navigationPreload.enable; warm-up detached, batched 8-way, skip-if-cached, `__precache-complete` marker; old app + SDK caches swept only after a complete warm-up

### `roster-cycle-data.js`
Raw roster cycle arrays only — `weeklyRoster`, `bilingualRoster`, `fixedRoster`, `cesRoster`, `dispatcherRoster`. Imported by `roster-data.js` only. A cell is `RD`/`OFF`/`SPARE`/`AL`/`RDW`/`HH:MM-HH:MM` or, Monday to Saturday, a BARE Other flavour (`fixedRoster[3]` is `TRG` Mon–Fri, a starter's training weeks, v25.40) — `validateRosterPatterns` in `roster-data.js` says why nothing else of the Other grammar may appear in a pattern. Edit here when the actual cycle patterns change (very rare). Do not import this file directly from app code — always go through `roster-data.js`.

### `roster-member-data.js`
The roster's PEOPLE and nothing else — `teamMembers`, one entry per member, no logic and no imports.
**Re-exported by `roster-data.js`**, which is where every consumer still reads it from: the app, the
`scripts/generate-roster-members.mjs` generator and `public-data-classification.test.mjs` all import
it from there unchanged.

Split out at v23.31 for the reason `roster-cycle-data.js` was: it is pure data, and its size guard
was measuring the wrong thing. This is the one table in the app whose length tracks **headcount** —
a new starter is a row here, a leaver is a flag here, and neither is a change to how anything works.
The ratchet cap on `roster-data.js` was last raised for a new starter (v23.26), whose own note said
it "will cross again on a future starter, and that is not drift"; a guard that fails on hiring
cannot also be watching for complexity.

**It is world-readable.** Both origins serve it with no session, no PIN and no token, so a field
added here is published the moment it ships — the classification argument, and what belongs in
Firestore behind a claim instead, is on `teamMembers` in CLAUDE.md. `public-data-classification.test.mjs`
fails on an unclassified field. Field-shape reference (`rosterChanges`, `proRatedAL`,
`bilingualContract`, …) lives in the comment block at the top of the file.

### `functions/index.js`
**Since v22.31 `parseRosterPDF` runs the PDF-grid witness** (`functions/roster-geometry.js`), awaited within a time budget BEFORE the model call since v24.04, and applies it LAST, after every model-side repair; the response gains `geometry` and `geometryRefused`.

The COMPOSITION ROOT of the Cloud Functions (domain split v20.55; this entry said "Eight Cloud
Functions" while eleven were deployed — the per-endpoint detail now lives with each domain module).
It defines the shared infrastructure — the four `defineSecret` params, `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` (pinned
here by sw-asset-check), `STAFF_SITE_URL`, `ADMIN_FUNCTION_ORIGINS`, `readRawBody` (the streaming
size-capped base64 body reader), `nowInLondon`, the size caps and the commit-ambiguous Firestore
code set — keeps TWO endpoints whose source is pinned to this file by static guards, and re-exports
the rest from the domain builders. **Its exports are the deploy surface** (Firebase discovers
functions from them); `functions-surface.test.mjs` pins every deployed name and fails on a drop or an
unreviewed addition.
- `parseRosterPDF` — admin upload → Claude AI → parsed shifts JSON (stays here: roster-prompt-parity.test.mjs reads the prompt from this file)
- `unlockCalendarViewer` (v20.12) — the staff Calendar PIN exchange (stays here: calendar-viewer-parity.test.mjs pins its handler source, secret binding and no-log rule to this file)
- `Object.assign(exports, buildDocumentEndpoints({...}))` / `buildAuthEndpoints({...})` / `buildOvertimeEndpoints({...})` — the wiring; the deps passed are the shared infra above, so the domain modules never own a second copy of a guarded literal

Both handlers that stayed here are EXECUTED by `index-endpoints.test.mjs`; `functions-surface.test.mjs`
proves only that they were defined.

### `index-endpoints.test.mjs`

**The two endpoints the domain split left in the composition root, driven for real.** Found by a
repo-wide mutation sweep: `parseRosterPDF` and `unlockCalendarViewer` were the only handlers in the
estate with no executing test at all, and six mutations survived both `npm test` and
`npm run test:functions` — the admin check reduced to `if (false)`, the PIN handler's two
fail-closed catches each continuing instead of answering 503, the v20.35 all-sources ceiling
deleted, `createCustomToken(uid, viewerClaims())` reduced to `createCustomToken(uid)`, and the
missing-or-malformed-secret guard reduced to `if (false)`.

This is the repo's named blind spot rather than a gap in the rules. `functions/calendar-viewer-auth.js`
is exemplary and every probe against it is caught; what nothing asked was whether the HANDLER calls
it, in the right order, and acts on the answer. The standing lesson is this very endpoint — v20.50
was signed off on a GET→405 and a wrong PIN→401, neither of which reaches the mint.

**Organised by what a wrong answer costs.** For the roster parser it is somebody else's API key and
the model's reading of whatever was uploaded, so every refusal — an ordinary member, the shared PIN
token, a manager, a truthy-but-not-`true` claim, no bearer, an unverifiable one — asserts that the
MODEL WAS NOT ASKED, against the same request a positive control proves is accepted. For the PIN it
is the whole roster against a 10,000-space secret: an uncounted guess is never answered, both
ceilings hold independently, concurrent guesses cannot all be compared, a correct PIN leaves nothing
behind, a viewer account somebody linked a credential to is rebuilt, the minted token carries `calendarViewer`
and none of `name`/`admin`/`manager`/`linksDesigner` by name, and a deployment fault is a 503 that
charges nobody.

`functions/index.js` is not a factory, so the fakes enter `require.cache` before it is required and
`getAuth`/`getFirestore` resolve a per-test world through a mutable binding — one load serves every
test. Every fake RECORDS: "refused with 403" and "spent the key and then returned 403" are the same
status line. Runs in `test:functions`; teeth-verified by eight mutations.

### `functions/documents.js`
The DOCUMENT-AND-NOTIFICATION domain (v20.55, second cut of the index.js split after push.js).
`buildDocumentEndpoints(deps)` — a factory taking the shared infra (secrets, VAPID public key,
`STAFF_SITE_URL`, `readRawBody`, `nowInLondon`, the caps) so index.js stays the composition root;
requires its own tools (admin, crypto, its helper subset, the push transport) directly. Returns:
- `getDocumentUrl` (v24.16) — POST `{ kind }` + a bearer ID token → a 15-minute signed URL `{ url, expiresAt, fileType, storagePath }` for the LATEST document of that kind. `storagePath` (v24.33) names the exact file signed, so the client can refuse a url for a newer upload than the document it is holding. The caller names a KIND, never a path (`functions/doc-url-core.js`), and the door mirrors the `firestore.rules` read. Answers 503 when signing fails, which is what a missing `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` grant on the runtime service account looks like.
- `ingestHuddle` — Power Automate → Firebase Storage + Firestore; timing-safe bearer auth, base64 validation, file-signature check, DOCX→HTML via lazy mammoth, atomic create-vs-notify transaction; awaits `pruneOldHuddles()` (3-month retention) before responding
- `onHuddleCreated` — Firestore `onDocumentCreated` trigger; fires the push fan-out for **manual** admin uploads (skips `uploadedBy === 'power-automate'`, which `ingestHuddle` already notified, to avoid double-notifying)
- `onCircularCreated` / `onNewsletterCreated` — `onDocumentCreated` triggers on `circulars` / `newsletters`; each fans out its own document-arrival push (📰 / 🗞️) via `buildPushPayload` + `fanOutPush`
- `sendPayReminderNotification` — scheduled (daily 08:00 London); on pay-cutoff Saturdays pushes a Pay Calculator reminder
- `sendTimetableReminder` — scheduled (daily 18:00 London); sends ONCE, the evening before `TIMETABLE_CHANGE` (Sat 12 Dec 2026): a broadcast, "🚆 New timetable from Sunday", landing on trains.html; its words are derived from the constant (v24.94, v24.96)
- Internal: `pruneOldHuddles(excludeDate)` (3-month sweep, Firestore first, prefix-swept Storage), the fan-out wrappers, and `HUDDLE_PUSH_PAUSED` (the huddle-push kill switch — lives HERE now)

### `functions/auth-endpoints.js`
The ACCOUNT-AND-CREDENTIAL domain (v20.55). `buildAuthEndpoints(deps)` — same factory pattern;
the pure decisions it orchestrates (claimsForTier, resolveRosterAuthConfig, computeOrphanLabels,
shouldRecordResetRequest, shouldNotifyAdmin, buildResetRequestNotice, summariseSignIns) live
unit-tested in `roster-parse-helpers.js`. Returns:
- `setupRosterAuth` — creates Firebase Auth accounts for all roster members; sets custom claims per tier (`{admin,name}` / `{manager,name}` / `{name}` + additive `linksDesigner`; admin outranks manager). **B4 (v16.30): the member + admin/manager/designer lists are SERVER-OWNED** — read from `roster-members.json` (generated from `roster-data.js`, CI-locked), not the request body; the body carries only action flags. Fails closed on empty admin/members config; role-holders are unioned into the processed set so a leaver sweep can never disable one. Leaver removal is dry-run by default (`removeOrphans` previews `orphansToDisable`; `confirmOrphanRemoval` disables + revokes refresh tokens). Re-run after adding a manager/designer so their claim lands
- `resetMemberPassword` (v18.63, PASSWORD_DESIGN Track C) — admin-only break-glass: verifies the caller's admin claim, resets a member's Firebase Auth password to their surname default (`nameToPassword`), optionally revokes their refresh tokens (`revoke`, default true), and stamps `passwordStatus.resetAt` (Admin SDK, bypasses rules); a stamp failure is a PARTIAL success (`stamped:false`), never a false "nothing changed". **It notifies the MEMBER (v23.62, owner request)** — `🔑 Password reset — set a new one`, to the uid the handler already resolved for the password write, via `sendTargetedPush`; the payload NEVER carries the new credential (a push renders on a lock screen, and the default is derived from a surname that is on the roster). A third independently-reported stage, `notified`, on the same rule as the two above it: a push failure must not report the reset as failed, and a member with no subscription is a legitimate `false` — which Operations turns into the admin's cue to tell them another way. CORS-restricted to `ADMIN_FUNCTION_ORIGINS`
- `requestPasswordReset` (v18.93–97) — the app's ONLY public unauthenticated endpoint: records a locked-out member's request (doc id = member name, so the collection is roster-bounded), transactional throttle, server-side kill switch, and the time-boxed `sendTargetedPush` admin notice with global coalescing
- `getSignInStats` (v18.96) — admin-only exact unique-account sign-in read from Firebase Auth `lastSignInTime`, allowlisted to the server-owned roster; returns four integers, no identity
- `getAccountSetupGaps` (v22.53) — admin-only READ comparing the server-owned roster against Firebase Auth: who has no account, whose account is disabled, whose claim tier disagrees, and which `@myb-roster.local` account is still enabled for somebody no longer on the roster. **Returns NAMES**, unlike `getSignInStats` — a count of provisioning gaps the admin cannot act on is worse than none, and folding it into that endpoint would break its own "four integers, no identity" contract. Grouped by REMEDY (`setUp` → press Set up accounts; `leavers` → the confirmed disable sweep), not by cause. Every judgement is in the pure `summariseAccountGaps`, including the refusal when no roster account is visible at all — a transient empty `listUsers` page would otherwise name the entire roster as unprovisioned

### `functions/calendar-viewer-auth.js`
The PURE server-side rules behind the staff Calendar PIN (v20.12) — CommonJS, split out of `index.js` so the security-relevant decisions are testable untangled from `req`/`res`. **Absent from this map until v21.63**: the client half (`calendar-access.js`) carried a long entry while the endpoint that mints the token had none.
- `isValidPinShape` · `pinMatches(a, b)` · `sourceKeyFor` / `clientIpOf` · `throttleDecision` / `recordFailure` / `isThrottleStateStale` · `reserveAttempt` / `refundAttempt` · `viewerAccountTamper` / `isViewerAccount` · `CALENDAR_VIEWER_UID` · `PIN_LENGTH` · `DEFAULT_THROTTLE` · `GLOBAL_SOURCE_KEY` / `GLOBAL_THROTTLE` · `viewerClaims()` (the one claim set the minted token carries).
- **Two properties govern every edit here, and the module header argues both:** the PIN is never learnt, stored, logged or returned (so there is no "which digit was wrong" and no input echo), and the throttle numbers are sized against a real tension rather than a template — 10,000 combinations on one side, the whole station behind ONE corporate NAT address on the other. Read `DEFAULT_THROTTLE`'s comment before changing a number in either direction.
- **Every attempt is charged BEFORE its compare, in the transaction that checks the block (`reserveAttempt`), and a right PIN is refunded after it (`refundAttempt`)** — Sep 2026 review: a plain read before the compare let concurrent guesses all be compared before any was counted. Only failures remain; a refund to zero deletes the row, so the store still cannot count usage. `viewerAccountTamper` names a shared account a PIN holder has linked a way in to (the unlock rebuilds it); `isViewerAccount` keeps Set up accounts and the leaver sweep off it. Tested by `calendar-viewer-auth.test.mjs`; the uid/claim agreement across browser ESM, CommonJS and `firestore.rules` is pinned by `calendar-viewer-parity.test.mjs`.

### `functions/push.js`
The web-push TRANSPORT (v20.52, extracted from `index.js`): lazy `web-push` require, one-per-instance VAPID setup, `fanOutPush` (everyone) and `sendTargetedPush` (named uids). Owns how a payload reaches a phone; never what it says or when — that is `buildPushPayload` in `roster-parse-helpers.js`. **`sendTargetedPush` fails CLOSED at every step** — no target uids → send nothing; a subscription doc with no `owner` → skipped, never assumed; no matches → log and stop. There is deliberately **no fall-back-to-everyone branch**, because the notification that needs it is "N. Surname is locked out". `releasePushForOwner(uid)` (whole-app review, Oct 2026) deletes the records one account owns — the confirmed leaver sweep's last step, because `fanOutPush` consults no owner and a leaver who never signed out kept receiving every broadcast. An empty uid deletes nothing and queries nothing.


### `functions/roster-geometry.js`
What the roster PDF's OWN GRID says about where each cell is, and the one refusal that follows from it (v22.31 — ROADMAP "Roster import" phase 1; proof in `experiments/roster-pdf-geometry/`). CommonJS. The only check in the import that does not come from the model: the table's rules are DRAWN, so cell occupancy is a physical fact the model cannot influence.
- `rulesFromOperatorList(ops, OPS)` → `{vx, hy}` — every stroked vertical/horizontal rule, clustered to 2px. They are `moveTo`/`lineTo` segments, NOT rectangles (the first experiment looked for rectangles and found zero rules on every page). Reads `OPS.constructPath`'s argument shape directly, which is why `pdfjs-dist` is pinned to **v4** (4.10.38): pdfjs changed the encoding at 5.1.91, and on 6.4.299 (measured 4 Nov 2026) nothing throws — every document reports `no-grid`, so the witness would stop SILENTLY and only the real-pdfjs test in `roster-geometry.test.mjs` notices. The module header holds the rewrite brief (target 6.5.438 or later, never 6.4.299).
- `assignRunsToGrid({items, vx, hy})` → rows `{name, cells[7], occupancy[7]}`, or `null` when the page does not carry exactly `GRID_COLUMNS` (9) vertical rules. PURE. Two hazards geometry introduces, both filtered: the **print footer** sits inside the grid (`Print Date … | Page 1 of 3` — caught by name AND by cell content, because one was not enough and the mutation proved it), and **header furniture** rows.
- `extractRosterGeometry(pdfBytes, {loadPdfjs?})` → `RosterGeometry` `{available, reason?, rows, pagesRead, pagesRejected}` — the ONLY code that touches pdfjs, dynamically imported. **Never throws**: pdfjs missing → `pdfjs-unavailable`; unopenable bytes → `unreadable-pdf`; no nine-column grid → `no-grid`; no text layer (a scanned sheet) → `no-text`. `isEvalSupported: false` so a PDF's font programs never reach `eval` (on the pinned v4; later pdfjs removed the option — see the module header). Empty pages are skipped by CONTENT, never by page number. **Bounded since v22.40** (external review): a `workBudgetMs` deadline checked BETWEEN pages stops a pathological file consuming the instance, and KEEPS the pages already read (`reason: 'work-budget'`), which `applyGeometryWitness` then reports as `partial` rather than throwing away as nothing.
- `awaitGeometryWithin(promise, ms?)` / `GEOMETRY_WAIT_BUDGET_MS` / `GEOMETRY_WORK_BUDGET_MS` (v22.40) — how long `parseRosterPDF` will WAIT for the geometry read (since v24.04 that wait sits BEFORE the model call rather than after it). **The budget is on the wait, not on the work**: the extraction starts before the model call and normally runs free inside its latency, so what needs bounding is only the tail — an optional witness that never settles would otherwise hold a finished parse until the function's own 120s timeout. Stopping the wait is not stopping the work (`fetch-timeout.js`'s rule, and here there are no side effects to leave half-done). A timeout returns the same fail-open shape, so it lands on `status: 'unavailable'` and reaches the admin through `geometryCopy` instead of being assumed away. A rejection — which this module is documented never to produce — fails open too.
- `matchGeometryRow(memberName, rows)` — exactly one row or `null`: letters-as-a-set first ("G. Miller" ≡ "Miller G" ≡ "Miller, G."), then a unique surname + initial ("Gareth Miller"). A duplicate at either tier is NO signal — `Vacant` appears three times on a real sheet.
- `geometryCoverage(stats, reviewedNames)` → `{ status, checked, total }` (v22.45) — the coverage figure the ADMIN reads, recounted over the rows that reach the review rather than over everything the model returned. `applyGeometryWitness` runs BEFORE hallucinated names are filtered (a refusal has to be stamped while the entry exists), and an invented name has no physical row, so it inflated the denominator and only the denominator: one real member fully checked beside one invention reported "1 of 2 — partial" and warned an admin about rows that had all been checked. Safe where the column cross-check deliberately is not, because a hallucinated name can only ever be `unmatched`, never `checked` — so recounting removes an unchecked row and can never add a checked one. `stats.ran` exists for the one trap: `unmatched` is empty on every early return, so without it a witness that never ran would recount to `complete`.
- `applyGeometryWitness(safeEntries, geometry, dates)` → `{status, refused, checked, total, unmatched}` — **THE RULE: an AI day the physical cell cannot support is REFUSED, not weighed.** A claim (anything but RD/OFF/BLANK/UNKNOWN) on an empty cell becomes `UNKNOWN|… this cell is empty on the PDF — the row may be a day out` and the member is listed in `refused`, so the client unticks the WHOLE row (the cells landing on occupied days are wrong too, invisibly). No repair, no second opinion. `status` is `complete` / `partial` / `unavailable` so a fail-open is visible on the response.
- **What it cannot see, stated:** a fully occupied week (nothing empty to contradict — `roster-alignment.js` stays for that), and `RD` written into an occupied cell (reading text is phase 3).
- `settledGeometry` is exported too (the fail-open shape a timed-out wait lands on).
- Wired in `functions/index.js`: started and awaited (bounded) BEFORE the model call since v24.04 — `functions/roster-prompt.js` — and applied LAST, after `applyColumnScanCrossCheck` and `applySundayScanCorrections`, because it only ever turns a value into UNKNOWN and so cannot reverse a repair. The response carries `geometry` and `geometryRefused` (filtered to known names). Tested by `roster-geometry.test.mjs` (test:functions) through the REAL pdfjs on a PDF the test writes by hand; teeth-verified by five mutations.

### `functions/roster-other-days.js`
What a roster cell reads as when it names an **Other-family day** — Training, Induction, Assessment, Team Day, Union course, Meeting (OTHER_DAYS.md). Requires nothing. Moved out of `normaliseShift` at v25.38, when meetings gained their times and `roster-parse-helpers.js` reached its line cap. A deliberate duplicate of the RECOGNITION side of the client grammar `FLAVOUR[" RDW"][" HH:MM-HH:MM"]` (`_OTHER_RE`, override-utils.js) — CommonJS cannot import a browser module.
- `OTHER_DAY_ALIASES` — the roster's words → the sentinel, an explicit table. **Each sentinel is also its own alias**: `MEET` was not until v25.38, and since the prompt asks the model for `MEET`, every meeting an obedient model returned became an unreadable review cell.
- `readOtherDayCell(s, workedRange)` — a bare flavour with an RDW marker on either side, or a MEETING with one time range in any order (`meetingWithTimes`) → the canonical value; null when the cell is not a clean Other-family cell. **A meeting is always a meeting** (owner, 6 Nov 2026). The other five keep the no-times rule.
- `namesMeeting(s)` — whether a cell names a meeting anywhere; `normaliseShift`'s trailing-content path refuses such a cell, so a meeting it could not read whole goes to review instead of becoming a plain shift.
- Tested by `roster-parse-helpers.test.mjs` (incl. that its sentinels equal the client's `OTHER_FLAVOURS`), through the whole handler on both paths by `index-endpoints.test.mjs`, and against the prompt's return values by `roster-prompt-parity.test.mjs`.

### `functions/roster-sunday-repair.js`
Layer 1 of the day-drift defence — the Sunday-anchored repair — and what happens to a Sunday it could not repair. Moved out of `roster-parse-helpers.js` at the v24.28 review, which was at its line cap. Requires `cell-day-rules.js` and `roster-parse-helpers.js` (for `reviewLabel`); nothing requires it but `functions/index.js`.
- `applySundayScanCorrections(entries, sundayScan, hasSundayCol, dates)` → `string[]` — Case A (the blank Sunday dropped, the row one day left: right-shifted back when the Saturday slot is EMPTY — `RD`, or since v22.25 the blank-Saturday question `buildSafeEntries` writes for `BLANK`) and Case B (an RDW marker stripped). A printed `OFF` Sunday is a rest day, never a Case A claim. When Saturday is occupied it can't reverse cleanly and **leaves the Sunday claim in place**, returning the member as DISPUTED — overwriting it with `RD` erased the one value the geometry witness could refuse.
- `settleDisputedSundays(entries, disputed, geoStats, dates, sundayScan)` — runs AFTER `applyGeometryWitness`: a disputed Sunday the witness refused is already a question; one the grid shows as occupied keeps the row read **only when the scan saw a literally empty cell** (a printed rest code — `NS`, `NA`/`N.A.`, `RD`, `-` — is ink the grid's "occupied" agrees with, so the row read's shift goes to review); one the witness could not look at (not run, or the row unmatched) becomes `UNKNOWN|…` for review. Wired in `functions/index.js`; the wiring is pinned by `index-endpoints.test.mjs`, the rules by `roster-parse-helpers.test.mjs`.

### `functions/roster-prompt.js`
The two roster-parse prompts, and the ONE vocabulary they share (v24.04 — ROADMAP "Roster import" phase 2). CommonJS. Phase 1 put a witness BESIDE the model's answer; this removes the model's freedom to get the day wrong at all.
- `SHIFT_VOCABULARY` — the code table, moved verbatim out of `functions/index.js`. **There must be exactly one copy**: `roster-prompt-parity.test.mjs` exists because a prompt once told the model to ignore a cell's second line while a status code lived there, and two prompts with two copies would leave one of them unguarded. That test now reads the table HERE, and asserts that BOTH prompts interpolate it.
- `buildCellTable(geometry, memberNames)` → `{usable, reason, rows, matched, unmatched}` — projects the geometry rows onto the members this roster covers. **All or nothing per document**: a member the grid missed would arrive with no cells, and "no cells" is indistinguishable from "a week of blanks" by `buildSafeEntries`, so one miss falls the whole document back to the PDF path. The caller passes `relevantNames` (already scoped by roster type) — an earlier draft gated on the whole `activeMembers` list, which can never match because it spans three roster types plus seven `hidden` Management accounts.
- `renderCellTable(rows)` — the table as the model sees it, each cell labelled with its day INSIDE the row, so a reordered or truncated response cannot be silently mis-seated.
- `buildCellPrompt(rows)` — the phase-2 prompt. States the prohibition first: the day of every cell is already decided and is not the model's to change. Sends no document block; the cells ARE the input.
- **Measured on three real rosters (WE 26/09/2026):** the phase-1 witness refuses only **19 of 47** simulated one-day-left misreads, because **23 of 47 rows work all seven days** and offer nothing to contradict. Phase 2 covers those by construction. Every one of the **55 distinct non-first-line cell values is a DUTY code** — no status code sits on a second line, because under the grid a leave cell is a ONE-LINE cell, so the original line-position defect stops existing rather than being caught.
- `reviewBlankReadsOfFilledCells(safeEntries, parsedMembers, cellRows, dates)` (whole-app review, H3) — geometry path only, right after `buildSafeEntries`: a day the model reported EMPTY (or left out) in a cell the grid shows holding something other than a rest marker becomes an UNREADABLE review cell naming what the cell holds. Without it a worked Sunday read as BLANK became `RD`, matched the base Sunday and never reached the review; the witness could not see it, because `RD` is not a claim. A cell that SAYS rest (`RD`, `OFF`, `SN`, `NA`, `NS`) is left alone, and nothing here counts as a witness refusal — one missed cell is not drift evidence and must not feed the breaker.
- `MODEL_REQUEST` (v25.28, moved out of `functions/index.js`, where it was `CLAUDE_MODEL` and an inline `thinking`) — HOW the model is asked, beside WHAT it is asked: `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `thinking: {type: 'between_tools'}`, `output_config: {effort: 'high'}`, `max_tokens: 16000`, frozen. Sonnet 5.5 has no `disabled` (a 400), and `between_tools` is accepted only alone and at effort `high` or below, so all three are pinned through the real handler by `index-endpoints.test.mjs`, since no stub raises the model's 400. The header carries the dated-snapshot check (4 Sep 2026) and the thinking history.
- `DECLINED_ERROR` (v25.28) — what the admin reads when the model declines (`stop_reason: 'refusal'`): a decline is not an unreadable answer, and a retry cannot change it.
- `DAY_LABELS` is exported too. Wired in `functions/index.js`: the geometry read is now awaited BEFORE the model call, and `cellTable.usable` picks the prompt. Tested by `roster-cell-read.test.mjs` (test:functions) and `roster-prompt-parity.test.mjs` (hygiene).

### `functions/roster-parse-helpers.js`
Pure helper functions — no Firebase, no HTTP, no secrets. Fully testable with Node's built-in test runner.
- `normaliseShift(raw)` — AI shift value → canonical app format (time, RDW pipe encoding, keywords). `NA`/`N/A`/`NS` → `RD` on every day (v25.38). **A meeting is always a meeting** (v25.38, owner: "MTG should always be other, with the meeting flavour"): `MEET` is its own alias — the prompt asks for it, and the parser had only known `MTG` and `MEETING`, so every obediently returned meeting reached the review as unreadable — and a meeting printed with its times keeps them, in either order (`10:00-14:00 MTG` → `MEET 10:00-14:00`). Both live in `functions/roster-other-days.js`. A cell naming a meeting that it cannot read cleanly goes to review: the trailing-content path asks `namesMeeting` and no longer takes the time and drops the code. The other Other flavours keep the no-times rule
- `buildWeekDates(weekEnding)` — Saturday ISO date → 7-date array Sun–Sat
- `extractAIJson(text)` — strips preamble/fences from AI response, returns parsed object
- `HEADER_TO_INDEX` — day-name → week-index map (0 = Sunday)
- `headerToDayIndex(header)` (v17.13) — resolves a raw AI header (trim + lowercase + `HEADER_TO_INDEX` lookup with a 3-char fallback) to a 0–6 index or `undefined`. SINGLE source for the three parse layers that resolve headers (`mapColumnHeadersToDates`, `buildSafeEntries`, `applyColumnScanCrossCheck`). Tested by `roster-parse-helpers.test.mjs`.
- `mapColumnHeadersToDates(headers, dates)` — returns `{ columnDates, error }` — validates and maps AI column headers to ISO dates
- `buildSafeEntries(parsedMembers, headers, dates)` — seeds each missing AI day through `blankCellMeaning` (`functions/cell-day-rules.js`: `RD` on a Sunday, an `UNKNOWN|…` review cell Mon–Sat, v22.19), normalises values
- ~~`flagUnmarkedSundayTimes`~~ — **REMOVED v22.19.** Its premise (a genuinely worked Sunday carries an RDW marker on the paper roster) was disproved by three real rosters: 21 worked Sundays, none marked; all 10 RDW markers on Mon–Sat. RDW means a REST DAY being worked, and Sunday is uncontracted. Beyond the false positives, it deleted the drift evidence the client's `detectShiftedRow` depends on — that detector ignores UNKNOWN cells, and on a left-shifted row the Sunday holds Monday's real value, the single strongest signal in the row. Recorded rather than deleted silently because the concern behind it is still live; the answer is the PDF's own grid (`experiments/roster-pdf-geometry/`), not a heuristic over the model's output.
- `applySundayScanCorrections` — MOVED to `functions/roster-sunday-repair.js` (v24.28 review); see that entry
- `isPayCutoffDay(date)` — mirrors isCutoffDate() from roster-data.js
- `nameToEmail(fullName)` / `nameToPassword(fullName)` — Firebase Auth credential derivation
- `fileSignatureMatches(buffer, fileType)` (v14.24) — magic-byte/file-signature check: verifies an uploaded buffer's leading bytes match its declared type (PDF `%PDF`, DOCX ZIP `PK`) before processing, so a mislabelled or hostile upload is rejected early
- Also exports the parse-review helpers (`applyColumnScanCrossCheck`, `normaliseScanValue`, `reviewLabel`, `parseStrictIsoDate`, and the cell-rule tokens/predicates it re-exports from `cell-day-rules.js`), the push helpers (`NOTIFICATION_FEATURES`, `buildPushPayload`, `shouldDeleteSubscription`, and `isAllowedPushEndpoint`/`PUSH_SERVICE_HOSTS` — v24.48, external audit F4: both senders in `functions/push.js` send only to an HTTPS, default-port, credential-free URL on a known push-service host, and SKIP (never delete) anything else) and the account/auth decisions the domain modules compose (`parseSetupActionFlags`, `resolveRosterAuthConfig`, `claimsForTier`, `computeOrphanLabels`, `summariseAccountGaps`, `shouldRecordResetRequest`, `buildResetRequestNotice`, `shouldNotifyAdmin`, `summariseSignIns`) — the `module.exports` block is the list.
- Covered by `roster-parse-helpers.test.mjs`

---

## What NOT to do

| Temptation | Why not |
|-----------|---------|
| Read `roster.data[week][day]` directly | Bypasses `startDate` suppression and Christmas rules. Always use `getBaseShift()`. |
| Add DOM access to `paycalc-calc.js` | It must stay importable by the Node test runner. |
| Add DOM access to `paycalc-roster-suggestions.js` | It must stay free of circular deps with `paycalc-app.js`. |
| Hardcode hex colours | Use CSS variables — every colour lives in `:root` in `shared.css` (the guide pages: in `guide-shell.css`'s own `:root`). |
| Import Firebase in `paycalc-calc.js` | Same reason as above — test-runner compatibility. |
| Use `alert()` | Use `console.error()` for dev errors; never show raw errors to staff. |
| Skip the version bump | Browsers will serve stale JS. Bump all places listed in `CLAUDE.md`. |

---

## Version bump checklist

See `CLAUDE.md` → "Version bumping (MANDATORY on every change)". 2 runtime locations (roster-data.js APP_VERSION + service-worker.js const; the 7 comment stamps were dropped v16.81); authoritative source is `APP_VERSION` in `roster-data.js`.

### `calendar-notif-prompt.js`
The Calendar's one-off **notification prompt strip** (`#notifPrompt`) and the **silent subscription renewal** for devices that already granted permission (v23.16 — extracted from `calendar-app.js` when the coordinator stood four lines under its ratchet; the IIFE closed over nothing the coordinator owns). Imports `notif.js`, `ls.js` and `storage-keys.js`; the auth promise and the access-type reader are injected.
- `initNotifPrompt({ authReady, getAccessType })` — permission granted → renew via `getNotifState()` and show nothing; never asked → show the strip once per device (`myb_notif_prompt_done`); denied → nothing. Both halves run for `named` access only (an anonymous `open` mode was admitted too until v24.34) and **never for the shared PIN viewer**, whose uid is the same on every office PC — a renewal there would re-stamp a real member's subscription `owner`. Gated on AUTH, not access: re-subscribing is a Firestore write.
- Two orderings the header pins: `notifSupported()` before any read of `Notification.permission` (a plain iOS Safari tab has no `Notification` global), and `requestPermission()` inside the tap before any `await` (iOS/WebKit refuse a no-gesture request).
- Wired by `calendar-app.js` BEFORE `initInstallPrompt`, which hides this strip when it shows its own — install first, because on iOS notifications need the installed app.

### `install-prompt.js`
The one-off **"add the roster to your home screen" strip** on the calendar (`.top-prompt`, the notification prompt's own slot). Shares that strip's markup, styling and slot so the two can never drift apart or, more importantly, **appear together** — and wired straight AFTER `initNotifPrompt`, which it then hides when it shows its own. Install outranks notifications because on iOS `notifSupported()` is FALSE until the app is installed, so the other strip's offer is not available yet.
- `initInstallPrompt({ accessReady, getAccessType })` — the access promise and the access-type reader are injected, as in `calendar-notif-prompt.js`.
- **The two platforms are different features, not one with a fallback.** Chromium fires `beforeinstallprompt` (single-use, and `preventDefault()` is required to keep it) → a real button. WebKit fires nothing and the only route is Share → Add to Home Screen → instructions and no button, shown on the app's own judgement rather than the browser's.
- **Three populations are never asked**, each for its own reason: the shared PIN viewer (a station, not a person), anyone already inside the installed app, and anyone on the Pages mirror — an offer there would create NEW installs on the origin the app is trying to leave.
- **Mobile-only is a BEHAVIOUR, not a layout.** The width test runs BEFORE the event is captured, because `preventDefault()` suppresses Chrome's own promotion — doing that where the strip is then invisible leaves a desktop member with no offer from anyone. The breakpoint is read off `--prompt-available` in `index.css` rather than copied here. The module header carries the argument, and the deliberate limit it does NOT rescue.
- **And the install NOTICE (v24.91, calendar-notices.js) asks through it.** `installRoute()` → `null` (a fine pointer, or the installed app) or `{ how: 'prompt'|'ios'|'menu', mirror }` — a touch screen is `(pointer: coarse)`, not the strip's width flag, and `how` is never `'prompt'` on the mirror. `promptInstall()` spends the ONE held offer, now module-level so the strip and the notice cannot hold two (call it first in a click handler). `yieldToInstallNotice()` takes the strip and the notification ask off screen for the visit, unflagged, and stops the strip showing later in it. `retireInstallStrip()` sets the strip's done flag: a member who answered the whole case is not asked again by the one-line version.

### `text-scale.js`
**How much larger than the stylesheet's sizes the phone actually draws its text**, and the two-tier stamp the calendar's control rows key on. Exists because Android's font/display scaling multiplies used font-sizes and leaves the viewport ALONE — so no width query and no narrower test viewport can reach the state where five buttons stop fitting, and the owner did not want two rows.
- `LARGE_FROM` (1.1) / `LARGE_VALUE` (`'large'`) · `COMPACT_FROM` (1.2) / `COMPACT_VALUE` (`'compact'`) · `tierFor(scale)` → `null` · `'large'` · `'compact'` · `isCompact(scale)` · `measureTextScale(doc)` · `applyTextScale(doc)` (stamps `data-text-scale` on `<html>`).
- **The probe IS the measurement**: a hidden 16px element's rendered height is the scale, whatever the OS calls the setting. **It cannot throw** — `calendar-app.js` calls it at module scope, and every fallback (no document, no body, a zero box, a bad seam) returns a value that is never `'compact'`.
- `'large'` tightens the month heading row; `'compact'` tightens the action row as well. **Compact is the step BEFORE the wrap, never instead of it.** Tested by `text-scale.test.mjs`; the probe itself in `e2e/calendar.spec.js`.

### `calendar-doc-access.js`
**May this Calendar read the documents?** (v23.17 — owner decision 7 Sep 2026: the Daily Huddle, the Weekly Retail Circular and the Marylebone Newsletter are not visible without the staff PIN or a password.) `firestore.rules` enforces that on the server; this is what makes the client honour it, for the reason `calendar-overrides.js` has a gate of its own: rules are evaluated server-side, and a read the local Firestore cache can answer never consults them. No imports.
- `hasDocumentAccess()` · `_resetDocumentAccessForTests()` (test seam: back to a freshly-loaded module) · `setDocumentAccess(open)` (only a literal `true` opens it — a truthy name or 1 is not access; subscribers are told once per CHANGE, never on a no-op) · `onDocumentAccess(fn)` → unsubscribe · `documentAccess` — the frozen `{ has, onChange }` handle the viewers take.
- **Opened by `calendar-app.js` in `onEveryGrant`** (every grant) and **shut** on every access-lost path (`onAccessLost`, the month-fetch handler). A deliberate Lock reloads, so the default applies.
- **Subscribers, not just a flag**, because the Huddle viewer's live subscription has to START when access arrives (a notification tap that landed on the PIN card opens the Huddle once the PIN is in — the deep link survives the unlock, AUTH_PLAN.md §5) and a Circular tap made while locked is finished when the reader unlocks.
- Consumers: `calendar-huddle-viewer.js` (`docAccess`: refuses the subscription at source while shut, tears it down and drops the data when it shuts, shows a locked message on a tap), `calendar-doc-viewer.js` (`docAccess`: locked message, no fetch, held key finished by the grant while the viewer is still open), `nav-panel.js` (`canReadDocuments`: refuses the tap before the blank tab is opened). Every other page leaves the defaults, which mean always-open.
